# WABV: Ezekiel

*Word Alive Bible Version, draft 1. Words in square brackets, such as \[H-RUACH\], point to entries in the Biblical Concepts and Language System. Footnotes and centre references are in the appendices at the end. Ezekiel is in the Hebrew mostly prose, with poetry in some of the oracles and laments (for example chapters 19, 26 to 28 and 32). The WABV text follows the Hebrew text and the Hebrew order of the book. The Greek text differs in places, and the footnotes record the matter where it affects the draft. The book is dated by the years of the exile of King Jehoiachin, which began in 597 before Christ, and the footnotes give the year in the modern count where the Hebrew gives a date. The dates are not always in the order of the chapters, and the WABV text does not rearrange them. The chapter and verse numbering is the English numbering. The book falls into eight parts in the WABV text, following the book's own movements: the vision and the call (chapters 1 to 3), the signs of the siege and the end (4 to 7), the vision of the temple and the departing glory (8 to 11), signs, false prophets and parables of judgement (12 to 19), the history of rebellion and the sword of the LORD (20 to 24), the oracles against the nations (25 to 32), the watchman, the shepherds and the dry bones (33 to 39) and the new temple and the land (40 to 48). The headings of the parts are not in the Hebrew. The WABV text does not decide on questions of the growth of the book, and the footnotes record only the language. The names of God are given as in the earlier books: "the LORD" for YHWH, "Lord" for Adonai, "the LORD of hosts" for YHWH tseva'ot, and, in the phrase that the book uses more than two hundred times, "the Lord GOD" for Adonai YHWH. The footnotes do not mark each place. The Hebrew phrase ben adam, which God uses of Ezekiel, is given as "son of man", and the footnote on 2:1 records the matter.*

## Part One: The Vision and the Call (Chapters 1 to 3)

### Chapter 1

**1** In the thirtieth year, on the fifth day of the fourth month, while I was among the exiles by the river Chebar, the heavens were opened, and I saw visions of God.

**2** On the fifth day of the month (it was the fifth year of the exile of King Jehoiachin),

**3** the word of the LORD came to the priest Ezekiel the son of Buzi, in the land of the Chaldeans by the river Chebar. And the hand of the LORD was on him there.

**4** I looked, and see, a stormy wind \[H-RUACH\] was coming from the north, a great cloud, with fire flashing continually and a brightness around it, and in the midst of it something like gleaming metal in the midst of the fire.

**5** And from the midst of it came the likeness of four living creatures. This was their appearance: they had the likeness of a human being,

**6** but each had four faces, and each had four wings.

**7** Their legs were straight, and their feet were like the feet of a calf, and they sparkled like burnished bronze.

**8** Under their wings on their four sides they had human hands, and all four had faces and wings.

**9** Their wings touched one another. They did not turn as they went. Each went straight ahead.

**10** As for the likeness of their faces, each had a human face, and each of the four had a lion's face on the right, and each of the four had an ox's face on the left, and each of the four had an eagle's face.

**11** Such were their faces. Their wings were spread upward. Each had two wings touching the wings of the one next to it, and two covering its body.

**12** Each went straight ahead. Wherever the spirit \[H-RUACH\] was to go, they went. They did not turn as they went.

**13** And in the midst of the living creatures was a likeness like burning coals of fire, like torches, moving to and fro among the living creatures. The fire was bright, and lightning went out from the fire.

**14** And the living creatures darted back and forth like the appearance of a flash of lightning.

**15** As I looked at the living creatures, I saw a wheel on the ground beside the living creatures, by each of their four faces.

**16** The appearance of the wheels and their making was like the gleam of beryl, and the four had the same likeness. Their appearance and their making was as if one wheel were within another.

**17** When they went, they went in any of their four directions. They did not turn as they went.

**18** Their rims were tall and awesome, and the rims of all four were full of eyes all around.

**19** When the living creatures went, the wheels went beside them, and when the living creatures were lifted from the ground, the wheels were lifted.

**20** Wherever the spirit \[H-RUACH\] was to go, they went, and the wheels were lifted beside them, for the spirit \[H-RUACH\] of the living creature was in the wheels.

**21** When they went, these went. When they stood, these stood. When they were lifted from the ground, the wheels were lifted beside them, for the spirit \[H-RUACH\] of the living creature was in the wheels.

**22** Over the heads of the living creatures was the likeness of a dome, shining like awesome crystal, stretched out above their heads.

**23** Under the dome their wings were stretched out straight, one toward the other. Each also had two wings covering its body.

**24** When they went, I heard the sound of their wings like the sound of many waters, like the voice of Shaddai, a sound of tumult like the sound of an army. When they stood, they let down their wings.

**25** There was a voice from above the dome over their heads. When they stood, they let down their wings.

**26** And above the dome over their heads was the likeness of a throne, in appearance like sapphire, and above the likeness of the throne was a likeness with the appearance of a human being, high above.

**27** And I saw something like gleaming metal, like the appearance of fire all around within it, from what looked like his waist upward, and from what looked like his waist downward I saw something like the appearance of fire, with a brightness around him.

**28** Like the appearance of the bow in the clouds on a rainy day, so was the appearance of the brightness all around. This was the appearance of the likeness of the glory of the LORD. When I saw it, I fell on my face, and I heard the voice of one speaking.

### Chapter 2

**1** He said to me, "Son of man, stand on your feet, and I will speak with you."

**2** As he spoke to me, the spirit \[H-RUACH\] entered into me and set me on my feet, and I heard the one who was speaking to me.

**3** He said to me, "Son of man, I am sending you to the people of Israel, to nations of rebels who have rebelled against me. They and their fathers have transgressed against me to this very day.

**4** The people are hard of face and stubborn of heart. I am sending you to them, and you will say to them, 'Thus says the Lord GOD.'

**5** Whether they listen or refuse to listen, for they are a rebellious house, they will know that a prophet has been among them.

**6** And you, son of man, do not be afraid of them, and do not be afraid of their words, though thistles and thorns are around you and you live among scorpions. Do not be afraid of their words, and do not be dismayed at their looks, for they are a rebellious house.

**7** You will speak my words to them, whether they listen or refuse to listen, for they are rebellious.

**8** But you, son of man, listen to what I say to you. Do not be rebellious like that rebellious house. Open your mouth and eat what I give you."

**9** I looked, and see, a hand was stretched out to me, and in it was a scroll.

**10** He spread it before me. It was written on the front and on the back, and written on it were lamentations and mourning and woe.

### Chapter 3

**1** He said to me, "Son of man, eat what you find here. Eat this scroll, and go, speak to the house of Israel."

**2** So I opened my mouth, and he gave me the scroll to eat.

**3** He said to me, "Son of man, feed your stomach and fill your belly with this scroll that I give you." Then I ate it, and in my mouth it was as sweet as honey.

**4** He said to me, "Son of man, go to the house of Israel and speak my words to them.

**5** For you are not sent to a people of foreign speech and a heavy tongue, but to the house of Israel,

**6** not to many peoples of foreign speech and a heavy tongue, whose words you cannot understand. If I had sent you to them, they would have listened to you.

**7** But the house of Israel will not be willing to listen to you, for they are not willing to listen to me, because all the house of Israel are hard of forehead and stubborn of heart.

**8** See, I have made your face as hard as their faces and your forehead as hard as their foreheads.

**9** Like diamond, harder than flint, I have made your forehead. Do not fear them, and do not be dismayed at their looks, for they are a rebellious house."

**10** Moreover he said to me, "Son of man, receive into your heart all my words that I will speak to you, and listen with your ears.

**11** And go to the exiles, to the sons of your people, and speak to them and say to them, 'Thus says the Lord GOD,' whether they listen or refuse to listen."

**12** Then the spirit \[H-RUACH\] lifted me up, and I heard behind me the sound of a great earthquake: "Blessed be the glory of the LORD from his place."

**13** It was the sound of the wings of the living creatures as they touched one another, and the sound of the wheels beside them, and the sound of a great earthquake.

**14** The spirit \[H-RUACH\] lifted me up and took me away, and I went in bitterness, in the heat of my spirit \[H-RUACH\], with the hand of the LORD strong on me.

**15** And I came to the exiles at Tel-abib, who lived by the river Chebar. And I sat there among them, overwhelmed, for seven days.

**16** At the end of seven days the word of the LORD came to me:

**17** "Son of man, I have made you a watchman for the house of Israel. When you hear a word from my mouth, you will warn them from me.

**18** If I say to the wicked, 'You will surely die,' and you do not warn him, or speak to warn the wicked from his wicked way to keep him alive, that wicked person will die for his iniquity, but his blood I will require from your hand.

**19** But if you warn the wicked, and he does not turn \[H-SHUV\] from his wickedness or from his wicked way, he will die for his iniquity, but you will have saved your life \[H-NEPHESH\].

**20** Again, if a righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] person turns \[H-SHUV\] from his righteousness and does wrong, and I put a stumbling block before him, he will die. Because you did not warn him, he will die for his sin, and his righteous deeds that he did will not be remembered, but his blood I will require from your hand.

**21** But if you warn the righteous person not to sin, and he does not sin, he will surely live, because he took warning, and you will have saved your life \[H-NEPHESH\]."

**22** And the hand of the LORD was on me there. He said to me, "Arise, go out to the valley, and there I will speak with you."

**23** So I arose and went out to the valley, and see, the glory of the LORD stood there, like the glory that I had seen by the river Chebar, and I fell on my face.

**24** But the spirit \[H-RUACH\] entered into me and set me on my feet, and he spoke with me and said to me, "Go, shut yourself in your house.

**25** And you, son of man, see, cords will be put on you, and you will be bound with them, so that you cannot go out among them.

**26** I will make your tongue cling to the roof of your mouth, so that you will be mute and unable to reprove them, for they are a rebellious house.

**27** But when I speak with you, I will open your mouth, and you will say to them, 'Thus says the Lord GOD.' Let the one who will listen, listen, and let the one who will refuse, refuse, for they are a rebellious house."

*Review note: Ezekiel 1 to 3 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the vision by the river Chebar (1:1-28), the call and the scroll (2:1 to 3:11), the return to the exiles at Tel-abib and the watchman (3:12-21), and the first command to be silent (3:22-27). The dates in 1:1-2 are the thirtieth year and the fifth year of the exile of King Jehoiachin (593 before Christ), and the footnote records the matter. The chapters are in prose, and the WABV text sets them as prose. In 1:4, 1:12, 1:20, 1:21, 2:2, 3:12, 3:14 and 3:24 ruach is tagged H-RUACH, with "stormy wind" in 1:4 and "spirit" elsewhere, and in 3:14 the word is used twice ("the spirit lifted me up ... in the heat of my spirit"). In 3:19 and 3:21 "your life" translates nafshekha and is tagged H-NEPHESH, and in 3:18 the Hebrew verb is "to keep him alive" (lehachayyoto), and the text is not tagged. In 3:19 and 3:20 "turn" and "turns" translate shuv and are tagged H-SHUV. In 3:20 "righteous" and "righteousness" are tagged H-TSEDAQAH. The phrase "son of man" (ben adam) is used throughout, and the rendering is open to the team's decision. The "likeness of a human being" at the throne (1:26) and the words on the glory of the LORD (1:28) are rendered as written. There are no departures from the Hebrew in chapters 1 to 3. The Hebrew is hard in some places, including 1:1, 1:4, 1:13, 1:22, 1:27, 2:6, 3:6, 3:12, 3:15 and 3:23. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Two: The Signs of the Siege and the End (Chapters 4 to 7)

### Chapter 4

**1** "And you, son of man, take a brick and set it before you, and engrave on it a city, Jerusalem.

**2** Put siegeworks against it, and build a siege wall against it, and heap up a ramp against it. Set camps against it, and place battering rams all around it.

**3** And you, take an iron plate and set it as an iron wall between you and the city. Set your face toward it, and it will be under siege, and you will besiege it. This is a sign for the house of Israel.

**4** Then lie on your left side and lay the iniquity of the house of Israel on it. For the number of the days that you lie on it, you will bear their iniquity.

**5** For I have assigned to you a number of days, three hundred and ninety days, equal to the years of their iniquity. So you will bear the iniquity of the house of Israel.

**6** And when you have completed these, you will lie down again, on your right side, and bear the iniquity of the house of Judah. I have assigned you forty days, a day for each year.

**7** And you will set your face toward the siege of Jerusalem, with your arm bared, and you will prophesy against it.

**8** See, I will put cords on you, so that you cannot turn from one side to the other until you have completed the days of your siege.

**9** And you, take wheat and barley, beans and lentils, millet and emmer, and put them in one vessel and make bread of them. For the number of the days that you lie on your side, three hundred and ninety days, you will eat it.

**10** Your food that you eat will be by weight, twenty shekels a day. From day to day you will eat it.

**11** And you will drink water by measure, the sixth part of a hin. From day to day you will drink it.

**12** And you will eat it as a barley cake, baking it in their sight on human dung."

**13** And the LORD said, "Thus the people of Israel will eat their bread unclean, among the nations where I will drive them."

**14** Then I said, "Ah, Lord GOD. See, my soul \[H-NEPHESH\] has not been defiled. From my youth up till now I have not eaten what died of itself or was torn by beasts, and no foul meat has come into my mouth."

**15** Then he said to me, "See, I give you cow dung instead of human dung, and you will prepare your bread on it."

**16** Moreover he said to me, "Son of man, see, I will break the staff of bread in Jerusalem. They will eat bread by weight and in anxiety, and they will drink water by measure and in dismay.

**17** This is so that they may lack bread and water, and look at one another in dismay, and waste away in their iniquity."

### Chapter 5

**1** "And you, son of man, take a sharp sword. Use it as a barber's razor, and pass it over your head and your beard. Then take scales for weighing and divide the hair.

**2** A third you will burn in the fire in the midst of the city, when the days of the siege are completed. A third you will take and strike with the sword all around the city. And a third you will scatter to the wind \[H-RUACH\], and I will unsheathe a sword after them.

**3** And you will take from these a small number and bind them in the skirts of your robe.

**4** And of these again you will take some and throw them into the midst of the fire and burn them in the fire. From there a fire will go out to all the house of Israel.

**5** Thus says the Lord GOD: This is Jerusalem. I have set her in the centre of the nations, with the countries all around her.

**6** And she has rebelled against my rules by acting more wickedly than the nations, and against my statutes more than the countries all around her, for they have rejected my rules and have not walked in my statutes.

**7** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: Because you are more turbulent than the nations that are all around you, and have not walked in my statutes or kept my rules, but have acted according to the rules of the nations that are all around you,

**8** therefore thus says the Lord GOD: See, I, even I, am against you. And I will execute judgements in your midst in the sight of the nations.

**9** And because of all your abominations I will do in you what I have not done, and the like of which I will never do again.

**10** Therefore fathers will eat their sons in your midst, and sons will eat their fathers. And I will execute judgements on you, and all of you who survive I will scatter to every wind \[H-RUACH\].

**11** Therefore, as I live, declares the Lord GOD, because you have defiled my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\] with all your detestable things and with all your abominations, I will also withdraw. My eye will not spare, and I will have no pity.

**12** A third of you will die of pestilence and be consumed by famine in your midst. A third will fall by the sword all around you. And a third I will scatter to every wind \[H-RUACH\], and I will unsheathe a sword after them.

**13** Thus my anger will spend itself, and I will vent my fury on them and satisfy myself \[H-NACHAM\]. And they will know that I, the LORD, have spoken in my jealousy, when I spend my fury on them.

**14** And I will make you a desolation and a reproach among the nations all around you and in the sight of all who pass by.

**15** You will be a reproach and a taunt, a warning and a horror, to the nations all around you, when I execute judgements on you in anger and fury and with furious rebukes. I, the LORD, have spoken,

**16** when I send against you the deadly arrows of famine, arrows for destruction, which I will send to destroy you, and when I bring more and more famine on you and break your staff of bread.

**17** I will send famine and wild beasts against you, and they will bereave you of your children. Pestilence and blood will pass through you, and I will bring the sword on you. I, the LORD, have spoken."

### Chapter 6

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "Son of man, set your face toward the mountains of Israel, and prophesy against them,

**3** and say, Mountains of Israel, hear the word of the Lord GOD. Thus says the Lord GOD to the mountains and the hills, to the ravines and the valleys: See, I, even I, will bring a sword on you, and I will destroy your high places.

**4** Your altars will be made desolate, and your incense stands will be broken, and I will throw down your slain before your idols.

**5** And I will lay the dead bodies of the people of Israel before their idols, and I will scatter your bones around your altars.

**6** Wherever you live, the cities will be waste and the high places ruined, so that your altars will be waste and ruined, your idols broken and destroyed, your incense stands cut down, and your works wiped out.

**7** And the slain will fall in your midst, and you will know that I am the LORD.

**8** Yet I will leave some of you alive. When you have among the nations some who escape the sword, when you are scattered through the countries,

**9** then those of you who escape will remember me among the nations where they are carried captive, how I was broken by their whoring heart that turned away from me and by their eyes that go whoring after their idols. And they will loathe themselves for the evils that they have done, for all their abominations.

**10** And they will know that I am the LORD. I did not say in vain that I would bring this disaster on them.

**11** Thus says the Lord GOD: Clap your hands and stamp your foot and say, Alas, for all the vile abominations of the house of Israel, for they will fall by the sword, by famine and by pestilence.

**12** The one who is far off will die of pestilence, and the one who is near will fall by the sword, and the one who is left and is spared will die of famine. Thus I will spend my fury on them.

**13** And you will know that I am the LORD, when their slain lie among their idols around their altars, on every high hill, on all the mountaintops, under every green tree and under every leafy oak, wherever they offered pleasing aroma to all their idols.

**14** And I will stretch out my hand against them and make the land desolate and waste, from the wilderness to Diblah, in all their dwelling places. Then they will know that I am the LORD."

### Chapter 7

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "And you, son of man, thus says the Lord GOD to the land of Israel: An end. The end has come on the four corners of the land.

**3** Now the end is on you, and I will send my anger against you. I will judge you according to your ways, and I will bring on you all your abominations.

**4** My eye will not spare you, and I will have no pity, but I will bring your ways on you, while your abominations are in your midst. Then you will know that I am the LORD.

**5** Thus says the Lord GOD: Disaster after disaster. See, it comes.

**6** An end has come. The end has come. It has awakened against you. See, it comes.

**7** Your doom has come to you, inhabitant of the land. The time has come. The day is near, a day of tumult and not of joyful shouting on the mountains.

**8** Now I will soon pour out my wrath on you, and spend my anger against you, and judge you according to your ways, and bring on you all your abominations.

**9** My eye will not spare, and I will have no pity. I will bring on you what your ways deserve, while your abominations are in your midst. Then you will know that I am the LORD who strikes.

**10** See, the day. See, it comes. Your doom has gone out. The rod has blossomed. Pride has budded.

**11** Violence has grown into a rod of wickedness. None of them will remain, nor their wealth, nor their abundance, and there will be no mourning for them.

**12** The time has come. The day has arrived. Let the buyer not rejoice, and let the seller not mourn, for wrath is on all their multitude.

**13** For the seller will not return to what he sold, as long as they live. For the vision concerning all their multitude will not be revoked, and because of his iniquity no one can maintain his life.

**14** They have blown the trumpet and made everything ready, but no one goes to battle, for my wrath is on all their multitude.

**15** The sword is outside, pestilence and famine are inside. The one who is in the field dies by the sword, and the one who is in the city is devoured by famine and pestilence.

**16** And if any survivors escape, they will be on the mountains like doves of the valleys, all of them moaning, each in his iniquity.

**17** All hands are feeble, and all knees turn to water.

**18** They put on sackcloth, and horror covers them. Shame is on all faces, and baldness on all their heads.

**19** They throw their silver into the streets, and their gold is like an unclean thing. Their silver and their gold cannot deliver them in the day of the wrath of the LORD. They cannot satisfy their hunger \[H-NEPHESH\] or fill their stomachs, for it was the stumbling block of their iniquity.

**20** His beautiful ornament he set for pride, and they made of it their abominable images and their detestable things. Therefore I make it an unclean thing to them.

**21** And I will give it into the hands of foreigners as prey, and to the wicked of the earth as spoil, and they will profane it.

**22** I will turn my face from them, and they will profane my treasured place. Robbers will enter it and profane it.

**23** Make the chain, for the land is full of bloody crimes and the city is full of violence.

**24** I will bring the worst of the nations to take possession of their houses. I will put an end to the pride of the strong, and their holy places \[H-QADOSH\] will be profaned.

**25** When anguish comes, they will seek peace \[H-SHALOM\], but there will be none.

**26** Disaster comes on disaster, and rumour follows rumour. They seek a vision from the prophet, but the teaching \[H-TORAH\] perishes from the priest, and counsel from the elders.

**27** The king mourns, the prince is wrapped in despair, and the hands of the people of the land are paralysed by terror. According to their way I will do to them, and according to their own judgements I will judge them, and they will know that I am the LORD."

*Review note: Ezekiel 4 to 7 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the signs of the siege (the brick and the iron plate, 4:1-3; the days on the left and the right side, 4:4-8; the bread by weight and measure, 4:9-17), the hair (5:1-4) and its word of judgement on Jerusalem (5:5-17), the word to the mountains of Israel (6:1-14) and the word on the end that has come (7:1-27). The chapters are in prose, apart from parts of chapter 7, which are in the rhythm of poetry, and the WABV text sets them as prose. In 4:14 "my soul" translates nafshi and is tagged H-NEPHESH. In 5:2, 5:10 and 5:12 "wind" translates ruach and is tagged H-RUACH. In 5:11 and 7:24 "sanctuary" and "holy places" are tagged H-QADOSH. In 5:13 "satisfy myself" translates ve-hinnachamti and is tagged H-NACHAM. In 7:19 "hunger" translates nafsham and is tagged H-NEPHESH, and in 7:13 "his life" translates chayyato, and the text is not tagged. In 7:25 "peace" is tagged H-SHALOM, and in 7:26 "teaching" is tagged H-TORAH. In 4:5 the WABV text gives "three hundred and ninety days" as the Hebrew has it, and the Greek has "a hundred and ninety", and the footnote records the matter. In 5:7 the WABV text gives "have acted according to the rules of the nations" as the Hebrew text has it, where the Greek and the Syriac have "have not acted", and the footnote records the matter. In 6:14 "Diblah" is given as the Hebrew has it, and many manuscripts and versions read "Riblah", and the footnote records the matter. The words on the fathers who will eat their sons (5:10) are rendered as written, with no comment. There are no departures from the Hebrew in chapters 4 to 7. The Hebrew is very hard in some places, including 4:5, 5:7, 6:9, 7:7, 7:10-11, 7:13, 7:16, 7:22 and 7:24. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Three: The Vision of the Temple and the Departing Glory (Chapters 8 to 11)

### Chapter 8

**1** In the sixth year, in the sixth month, on the fifth day of the month, as I sat in my house, with the elders of Judah sitting before me, the hand of the Lord GOD fell on me there.

**2** I looked, and see, a likeness with the appearance of a man. From what looked like his waist downward there was fire, and from his waist upward there was something like the appearance of brightness, like gleaming metal.

**3** He stretched out the form of a hand and took me by a lock of my head, and the spirit \[H-RUACH\] lifted me up between earth and heaven and brought me in visions of God to Jerusalem, to the entrance of the gateway of the inner court that faces north, where the seat of the image of jealousy, which provokes jealousy, stood.

**4** And see, the glory of the God of Israel was there, like the vision that I saw in the valley.

**5** Then he said to me, "Son of man, lift up your eyes toward the north." So I lifted up my eyes toward the north, and see, north of the altar gate, in the entrance, was this image of jealousy.

**6** And he said to me, "Son of man, do you see what they are doing, the great abominations that the house of Israel are doing here, to drive me far from my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\]? But you will see still greater abominations."

**7** And he brought me to the entrance of the court, and I looked, and see, there was a hole in the wall.

**8** Then he said to me, "Son of man, dig in the wall." So I dug in the wall, and see, there was a doorway.

**9** And he said to me, "Go in, and see the vile abominations that they are doing here."

**10** So I went in and saw. And see, every form of creeping thing and loathsome beast, and all the idols of the house of Israel, were carved on the wall all around.

**11** And before them stood seventy of the elders of the house of Israel, with Jaazaniah the son of Shaphan standing among them. Each had his censer in his hand, and the smoke of the cloud of incense went up.

**12** Then he said to me, "Son of man, have you seen what the elders of the house of Israel are doing in the dark, each in his room of pictures? For they say, 'The LORD does not see us. The LORD has forsaken the land.'"

**13** He said also to me, "You will see still greater abominations that they are doing."

**14** Then he brought me to the entrance of the north gate of the house of the LORD, and see, women were sitting there weeping for Tammuz.

**15** Then he said to me, "Have you seen this, son of man? You will see still greater abominations than these."

**16** And he brought me into the inner court of the house of the LORD. And see, at the entrance of the temple of the LORD, between the porch and the altar, were about twenty-five men, with their backs to the temple of the LORD and their faces to the east, bowing down to the sun toward the east.

**17** Then he said to me, "Have you seen this, son of man? Is it too light a thing for the house of Judah to do the abominations that they do here, that they should fill the land with violence and provoke me again to anger? See, they put the branch to their nose.

**18** Therefore I will act in wrath. My eye will not spare, and I will have no pity. And though they cry in my ears with a loud voice, I will not listen to them."

### Chapter 9

**1** Then he cried in my ears with a loud voice, "Bring near the executioners of the city, each with his weapon of destruction in his hand."

**2** And see, six men came from the direction of the upper gate, which faces north, each with his weapon for slaughter in his hand, and with them was a man clothed in linen, with a writing case at his waist. They went in and stood beside the bronze altar.

**3** Now the glory of the God of Israel had gone up from the cherub on which it rested to the threshold of the house. And he called to the man clothed in linen, who had the writing case at his waist.

**4** And the LORD said to him, "Pass through the city, through Jerusalem, and put a mark on the foreheads of the people who groan and sigh over all the abominations that are done in it."

**5** And to the others he said in my hearing, "Pass through the city after him, and strike. Your eye will not spare, and you will show no pity.

**6** Kill old men outright, young men and young women, little children and women, but touch no one on whom is the mark. And begin at my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\]." So they began with the elders who were before the house.

**7** Then he said to them, "Defile the house, and fill the courts with the slain. Go out." So they went out and struck in the city.

**8** And while they were striking, and I was left alone, I fell on my face and cried, "Ah, Lord GOD. Will you destroy all the remnant of Israel in the pouring out of your wrath on Jerusalem?"

**9** Then he said to me, "The guilt of the house of Israel and Judah is exceedingly great. The land is full of blood, and the city is full of injustice. For they say, 'The LORD has forsaken the land, and the LORD does not see.'

**10** As for me, my eye will not spare, and I will have no pity. I will bring their conduct on their heads."

**11** And see, the man clothed in linen, with the writing case at his waist, brought back word, saying, "I have done as you commanded me."

### Chapter 10

**1** Then I looked, and see, on the dome that was over the heads of the cherubim there appeared something like sapphire, in appearance like a throne.

**2** And he said to the man clothed in linen, "Go in among the whirling wheels underneath the cherubim, and fill your hands with burning coals from between the cherubim, and scatter them over the city." And he went in as I looked.

**3** Now the cherubim were standing on the south side of the house when the man went in, and the cloud filled the inner court.

**4** And the glory of the LORD went up from the cherub to the threshold of the house, and the house was filled with the cloud, and the court was filled with the brightness of the glory of the LORD.

**5** And the sound of the wings of the cherubim was heard as far as the outer court, like the voice of God Almighty when he speaks.

**6** And when he commanded the man clothed in linen, "Take fire from between the whirling wheels, from between the cherubim," he went in and stood beside a wheel.

**7** And a cherub stretched out his hand from between the cherubim to the fire that was between the cherubim, and took some of it and put it into the hands of the man clothed in linen, who took it and went out.

**8** The cherubim appeared to have the form of a human hand under their wings.

**9** And I looked, and see, there were four wheels beside the cherubim, one wheel beside each cherub, and the appearance of the wheels was like gleaming beryl.

**10** And as for their appearance, the four had the same likeness, as if one wheel were within another.

**11** When they went, they went in any of their four directions without turning as they went, but in whatever direction the front looked, they followed it without turning as they went.

**12** And their whole body, their backs, their hands, their wings and the wheels were full of eyes all around, the wheels that the four of them had.

**13** As for the wheels, they were called in my hearing "the whirling wheels."

**14** And each had four faces. The first face was the face of a cherub, and the second face was a human face, and the third the face of a lion, and the fourth the face of an eagle.

**15** And the cherubim mounted up. These were the living creatures that I saw by the river Chebar.

**16** And when the cherubim went, the wheels went beside them. And when the cherubim lifted up their wings to mount up from the ground, the wheels did not turn from beside them.

**17** When they stood still, these stood still, and when they mounted up, these mounted up with them, for the spirit \[H-RUACH\] of the living creature was in them.

**18** Then the glory of the LORD went out from the threshold of the house and stood over the cherubim.

**19** And the cherubim lifted up their wings and mounted up from the ground before my eyes as they went out, with the wheels beside them. And they stood at the entrance of the east gate of the house of the LORD, and the glory of the God of Israel was over them.

**20** These were the living creatures that I saw underneath the God of Israel by the river Chebar, and I knew that they were cherubim.

**21** Each had four faces, and each four wings, and underneath their wings the likeness of human hands.

**22** And as for the likeness of their faces, they were the same faces whose appearance I had seen by the river Chebar. Each went straight ahead.

### Chapter 11

**1** The spirit \[H-RUACH\] lifted me up and brought me to the east gate of the house of the LORD, which faces east. And see, at the entrance of the gateway there were twenty-five men. And I saw among them Jaazaniah the son of Azzur and Pelatiah the son of Benaiah, officials of the people.

**2** And he said to me, "Son of man, these are the men who devise iniquity and who give wicked counsel in this city,

**3** who say, 'The time is not near to build houses. This city is the cooking pot, and we are the meat.'

**4** Therefore prophesy against them. Prophesy, son of man."

**5** And the Spirit \[H-RUACH\] of the LORD fell on me, and he said to me, "Say, Thus says the LORD: So you think, house of Israel. For I know the things that come into your mind.

**6** You have multiplied your slain in this city and have filled its streets with the slain.

**7** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: Your slain whom you have laid in the midst of it, they are the meat, and this city is the pot, but you will be taken out of the midst of it.

**8** You have feared the sword, and the sword I will bring on you," declares the Lord GOD.

**9** "And I will bring you out of the midst of it and give you into the hands of foreigners, and execute judgements on you.

**10** You will fall by the sword. I will judge you at the border of Israel, and you will know that I am the LORD.

**11** This city will not be your pot, nor will you be the meat in the midst of it. I will judge you at the border of Israel,

**12** and you will know that I am the LORD. For you have not walked in my statutes or kept my rules, but have acted according to the rules of the nations that are around you."

**13** And while I was prophesying, Pelatiah the son of Benaiah died. Then I fell down on my face and cried out with a loud voice and said, "Ah, Lord GOD. Will you make a full end of the remnant of Israel?"

**14** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**15** "Son of man, your brothers, even your brothers, your kinsmen, the whole house of Israel, all of them, are those of whom the inhabitants of Jerusalem have said, 'Go far from the LORD. To us this land is given for a possession.'

**16** Therefore say, Thus says the Lord GOD: Though I removed them far off among the nations, and though I scattered them among the countries, yet I have been a sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\] to them for a little while in the countries where they have gone.

**17** Therefore say, Thus says the Lord GOD: I will gather you from the peoples and assemble you out of the countries where you have been scattered, and I will give you the land of Israel.

**18** And when they come there, they will remove from it all its detestable things and all its abominations.

**19** And I will give them one heart, and a new spirit \[H-RUACH\] I will put within them. I will remove the heart of stone from their flesh and give them a heart of flesh,

**20** that they may walk in my statutes and keep my rules and obey them. And they will be my people, and I will be their God.

**21** But as for those whose heart goes after their detestable things and their abominations, I will bring their deeds on their own heads," declares the Lord GOD.

**22** Then the cherubim lifted up their wings, with the wheels beside them, and the glory of the God of Israel was over them.

**23** And the glory of the LORD went up from the midst of the city and stood on the mountain that is on the east side of the city.

**24** And the spirit \[H-RUACH\] lifted me up and brought me in the vision by the Spirit \[H-RUACH\] of God into Chaldea, to the exiles. Then the vision that I had seen went up from me.

**25** And I told the exiles all the things that the LORD had shown me.

*Review note: Ezekiel 8 to 11 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the vision of the abominations in the temple (8:1-18), the executioners and the man clothed in linen who marks the foreheads (9:1-11), the cherubim, the wheels and the coals of fire (10:1-22), and the twenty-five men, the word of the LORD against the city and for the exiles, and the glory that leaves the city (11:1-25). The vision is dated to the sixth year, on the fifth day of the sixth month (8:1), which is September 592 before Christ, and the footnote records the matter. The chapters are in prose, and the WABV text sets them as prose. In 8:3, 10:17, 11:1, 11:19 and 11:24 ruach is tagged H-RUACH, and in 11:5 and 11:24 the phrases "the Spirit of the LORD" and "the Spirit of God" have the capital letter in the draft; the capital letter is the draft's choice, and the team should decide it with Isaiah 63:10-11 and the other uses of ruach. In 8:6, 9:6 and 11:16 "sanctuary" translates miqdash and is tagged H-QADOSH. In 8:17 "provoke me again" translates the verb shuv used with another verb, and the text is not tagged. In 11:19 "one heart" is given as the Hebrew has it, and the footnote records that many manuscripts and the versions read "a new heart", as in 36:26. In 10:14 "the face of a cherub" stands where 1:10 has "an ox", and the footnote records the matter. In 10:5 "God Almighty" translates El Shaddai. The slaughter in 9:5-7 is rendered as written, with no comment. At 8:2 the WABV text gives "the appearance of a man", following the Greek and the Syriac, where the Hebrew text has "the appearance of fire", and it departs from the Hebrew; it is the one departure in chapters 8 to 11, and it is flagged for the scholar. The Hebrew is hard in some places, including 8:2, 8:3, 8:12, 8:17, 10:12, 11:3, 11:15 and 11:16. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Four: Signs, False Prophets and Parables of Judgement (Chapters 12 to 19)

### Chapter 12

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "Son of man, you live in the midst of a rebellious house, who have eyes to see but do not see, who have ears to hear but do not hear, for they are a rebellious house.

**3** As for you, son of man, prepare for yourself an exile's baggage, and go into exile by day in their sight. You will go like an exile from your place to another place in their sight. Perhaps they will understand, though they are a rebellious house.

**4** You will bring out your baggage by day in their sight, as baggage for exile. And you yourself will go out in the evening in their sight, as those do who go into exile.

**5** In their sight dig through the wall, and go out through it.

**6** In their sight you will lift your baggage on your shoulder and carry it out at dusk. You will cover your face so that you cannot see the land, for I have made you a sign for the house of Israel."

**7** I did as I was commanded. I brought out my baggage by day, as baggage for exile, and in the evening I dug through the wall with my own hands. I brought it out at dusk, carrying it on my shoulder in their sight.

**8** In the morning the word of the LORD came to me:

**9** "Son of man, has not the house of Israel, the rebellious house, said to you, 'What are you doing?'

**10** Say to them, 'Thus says the Lord GOD: This oracle concerns the prince in Jerusalem and all the house of Israel who are in it.'

**11** Say, 'I am a sign for you. As I have done, so it will be done to them. They will go into exile, into captivity.

**12** And the prince who is among them will lift his baggage on his shoulder in the dark and go out. They will dig through the wall to bring him out through it. He will cover his face, so that he cannot see the land with his eyes.

**13** And I will spread my net over him, and he will be taken in my snare. And I will bring him to Babylon, the land of the Chaldeans, yet he will not see it, and he will die there.

**14** And I will scatter to every wind \[H-RUACH\] all who are around him, his helpers and all his troops, and I will unsheathe a sword after them.

**15** And they will know that I am the LORD, when I disperse them among the nations and scatter them through the countries.

**16** But I will let a few of them escape from the sword, from famine and pestilence, that they may declare all their abominations among the nations where they go, and may know that I am the LORD.'"

**17** The word of the LORD came to me:

**18** "Son of man, eat your bread with quaking, and drink your water with trembling and with anxiety.

**19** And say to the people of the land, Thus says the Lord GOD concerning the inhabitants of Jerusalem in the land of Israel: They will eat their bread with anxiety and drink their water in dismay. In this way her land will be stripped of all it contains, because of the violence of all who live in it.

**20** And the inhabited cities will be laid waste, and the land will become a desolation, and you will know that I am the LORD."

**21** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**22** "Son of man, what is this proverb that you have in the land of Israel, saying, 'The days are prolonged, and every vision comes to nothing'?

**23** Tell them therefore, 'Thus says the Lord GOD: I will put an end to this proverb, and they will no more use it as a proverb in Israel.' But say to them, 'The days are near, and the fulfilment of every vision.

**24** For there will be no more any false vision or flattering divination within the house of Israel.

**25** For I am the LORD. I will speak the word that I will speak, and it will be performed. It will no longer be delayed. In your days, rebellious house, I will speak the word and perform it,' declares the Lord GOD."

**26** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**27** "Son of man, see, the house of Israel say, 'The vision that he sees is for many days from now, and he prophesies of times far off.'

**28** Therefore say to them, Thus says the Lord GOD: None of my words will be delayed any longer. The word that I speak will be performed," declares the Lord GOD.

### Chapter 13

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "Son of man, prophesy against the prophets of Israel who are prophesying, and say to those who prophesy from their own hearts: Hear the word of the LORD.

**3** Thus says the Lord GOD: Woe to the foolish prophets who follow their own spirit \[H-RUACH\] and have seen nothing.

**4** Your prophets have been like jackals among ruins, Israel.

**5** You have not gone up into the breaches or built up a wall for the house of Israel, that it might stand in battle in the day of the LORD.

**6** They have seen false visions and lying divination. They say, 'Declares the LORD,' when the LORD has not sent them, and yet they expect him to fulfil their word.

**7** Have you not seen a false vision and uttered a lying divination, whenever you have said, 'Declares the LORD,' although I have not spoken?

**8** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: Because you have uttered falsehood and seen lies, therefore see, I am against you," declares the Lord GOD.

**9** "My hand will be against the prophets who see false visions and who give lying divinations. They will not be in the council of my people, nor be written in the register of the house of Israel, nor will they enter the land of Israel. And you will know that I am the Lord GOD.

**10** Precisely because they have misled my people, saying, 'Peace \[H-SHALOM\],' when there is no peace, and because, when the people build a wall, these prophets smear it with whitewash,

**11** say to those who smear it with whitewash that it will fall. There will be a deluge of rain, and great hailstones will fall, and a stormy wind \[H-RUACH\] will break out.

**12** And when the wall falls, will it not be said to you, 'Where is the coating with which you smeared it?'

**13** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: I will make a stormy wind \[H-RUACH\] break out in my wrath, and there will be a deluge of rain in my anger, and great hailstones in wrath to make a full end.

**14** And I will break down the wall that you have smeared with whitewash, and bring it down to the ground, so that its foundation will be laid bare. When it falls, you will perish in the midst of it, and you will know that I am the LORD.

**15** Thus I will spend my wrath on the wall and on those who have smeared it with whitewash, and I will say to you, The wall is no more, nor those who smeared it,

**16** the prophets of Israel who prophesied concerning Jerusalem and saw visions of peace \[H-SHALOM\] for her when there was no peace," declares the Lord GOD.

**17** "And you, son of man, set your face against the daughters of your people, who prophesy out of their own hearts. Prophesy against them

**18** and say, Thus says the Lord GOD: Woe to the women who sew magic bands on all wrists and make veils for the heads of people of every stature, in the hunt for souls \[H-NEPHESH\]. Will you hunt down the souls \[H-NEPHESH\] of my people and keep your own souls \[H-NEPHESH\] alive?

**19** You have profaned me among my people for handfuls of barley and for pieces of bread, putting to death souls \[H-NEPHESH\] who should not die and keeping alive souls \[H-NEPHESH\] who should not live, by your lying to my people, who listen to lies.

**20** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: See, I am against your magic bands with which you hunt souls \[H-NEPHESH\] like birds, and I will tear them from your arms, and I will let the souls \[H-NEPHESH\] go free, the souls that you hunt like birds.

**21** Your veils also I will tear off and deliver my people out of your hand, and they will be no more in your hand as prey, and you will know that I am the LORD.

**22** Because you have disheartened the righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] falsely, although I have not grieved him, and you have encouraged the wicked, that he should not turn \[H-SHUV\] from his evil way to keep him alive,

**23** therefore you will no more see false visions or practise divination. I will deliver my people out of your hand. And you will know that I am the LORD."

### Chapter 14

**1** Then certain of the elders of Israel came to me and sat before me.

**2** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**3** "Son of man, these men have taken their idols into their hearts and set the stumbling block of their iniquity before their faces. Should I let myself be consulted by them at all?

**4** Therefore speak to them and say to them, Thus says the Lord GOD: Any of the house of Israel who takes his idols into his heart and sets the stumbling block of his iniquity before his face, and yet comes to the prophet, I the LORD will answer him as he comes with the multitude of his idols,

**5** that I may lay hold of the hearts of the house of Israel, who are all estranged from me through their idols.

**6** Therefore say to the house of Israel, Thus says the Lord GOD: Return \[H-SHUV\] and turn \[H-SHUV\] away from your idols, and turn \[H-SHUV\] away your faces from all your abominations.

**7** For any of the house of Israel, or of the foreigners who live in Israel, who separates himself from me, taking his idols into his heart and putting the stumbling block of his iniquity before his face, and yet comes to a prophet to consult me through him, I the LORD will answer him myself.

**8** And I will set my face against that person. I will make him a sign and a byword and cut him off from the midst of my people, and you will know that I am the LORD.

**9** And if the prophet is deceived and speaks a word, I, the LORD, have deceived that prophet, and I will stretch out my hand against him and will destroy him from the midst of my people Israel.

**10** And they will bear their punishment. The punishment of the prophet and the punishment of the inquirer will be alike,

**11** that the house of Israel may no more go astray from me, nor defile themselves any more with all their transgressions, but that they may be my people and I may be their God," declares the Lord GOD.

**12** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**13** "Son of man, when a land sins against me by acting faithlessly, and I stretch out my hand against it and break its staff of bread and send famine on it, and cut off from it human and animal,

**14** even if these three men, Noah, Daniel and Job, were in it, they would save only their own lives \[H-NEPHESH\] by their righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\]," declares the Lord GOD.

**15** "If I cause wild beasts to pass through the land, and they ravage it, and it is made desolate, so that no one may pass through because of the beasts,

**16** even if these three men were in it, as I live," declares the Lord GOD, "they would save neither sons nor daughters. They alone would be saved, but the land would be desolate.

**17** Or if I bring a sword on that land and say, Let a sword pass through the land, and I cut off from it human and animal,

**18** though these three men were in it, as I live," declares the Lord GOD, "they would save neither sons nor daughters, but they alone would be saved.

**19** Or if I send a pestilence into that land and pour out my wrath on it with blood, to cut off from it human and animal,

**20** even if Noah, Daniel and Job were in it, as I live," declares the Lord GOD, "they would save neither son nor daughter. They would save only their own lives \[H-NEPHESH\] by their righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\].

**21** For thus says the Lord GOD: How much more when I send on Jerusalem my four disastrous judgements, sword, famine, wild beasts and pestilence, to cut off from it human and animal.

**22** But see, some survivors will be left in it, sons and daughters who will be brought out. See, when they come out to you, and you see their ways and their deeds, you will be consoled \[H-NACHAM\] for the disaster that I have brought on Jerusalem, for all that I have brought on it.

**23** They will console \[H-NACHAM\] you, when you see their ways and their deeds, and you will know that I did not do without cause all that I have done in it," declares the Lord GOD.

### Chapter 15

**1** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "Son of man, how is the wood of the vine more than any wood, the vine branch that is among the trees of the forest?

**3** Is wood taken from it to make anything? Do people take a peg from it to hang any vessel on it?

**4** See, it is given to the fire for fuel. When the fire has consumed both ends of it, and the middle of it is charred, is it useful for anything?

**5** See, when it was whole, it was used for nothing. How much less, when the fire has consumed it and it is charred, can it still be used for anything?

**6** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: Like the wood of the vine among the trees of the forest, which I have given to the fire for fuel, so I will give up the inhabitants of Jerusalem.

**7** And I will set my face against them. Though they escape from the fire, the fire will yet consume them, and you will know that I am the LORD, when I set my face against them.

**8** And I will make the land desolate, because they have acted faithlessly," declares the Lord GOD.

### Chapter 16

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "Son of man, make known to Jerusalem her abominations,

**3** and say, Thus says the Lord GOD to Jerusalem: Your origin and your birth are from the land of the Canaanites. Your father was an Amorite and your mother a Hittite.

**4** As for your birth, on the day you were born your cord was not cut, nor were you washed with water to cleanse you, nor rubbed with salt, nor wrapped in cloths.

**5** No eye pitied you, to do any of these things to you out of compassion for you, but you were cast out on the open field, for you were abhorred, on the day that you were born.

**6** And when I passed by you and saw you wallowing in your blood, I said to you in your blood, 'Live.' I said to you in your blood, 'Live.'

**7** I made you flourish like a plant of the field. And you grew up and became tall and arrived at full adornment. Your breasts were formed, and your hair had grown, yet you were naked and bare.

**8** When I passed by you again and saw you, see, you were at the age for love, and I spread the corner of my garment over you and covered your nakedness. I made my vow to you and entered into a covenant \[H-BERIT\] with you," declares the Lord GOD, "and you became mine.

**9** Then I bathed you with water and washed off your blood from you and anointed you with oil.

**10** I clothed you also with embroidered cloth and shod you with fine leather. I wrapped you in fine linen and covered you with silk.

**11** And I adorned you with ornaments and put bracelets on your wrists and a chain on your neck.

**12** And I put a ring on your nose and earrings in your ears and a beautiful crown on your head.

**13** Thus you were adorned with gold and silver, and your clothing was of fine linen, silk and embroidered cloth. You ate fine flour and honey and oil. You grew exceedingly beautiful and advanced to royalty.

**14** And your renown went out among the nations because of your beauty, for it was perfect through the splendour that I had bestowed on you," declares the Lord GOD.

**15** "But you trusted in your beauty and played the prostitute because of your renown, and lavished your whorings on any passerby. Your beauty became his.

**16** You took some of your garments and made for yourself colourful shrines, and on them you played the prostitute. The like has never been, and will never be.

**17** You also took your beautiful jewels of my gold and of my silver, which I had given you, and made for yourself images of men, and with them you played the prostitute.

**18** And you took your embroidered garments to cover them, and set my oil and my incense before them.

**19** Also my food that I gave you (I fed you with fine flour and oil and honey) you set before them for a pleasing aroma. And so it was," declares the Lord GOD.

**20** "And you took your sons and your daughters, whom you had borne to me, and these you sacrificed to them to be devoured. Were your whorings so small a matter

**21** that you slaughtered my children and delivered them up as an offering by fire to them?

**22** And in all your abominations and your whorings you did not remember the days of your youth, when you were naked and bare, wallowing in your blood.

**23** And after all your wickedness (woe, woe to you," declares the Lord GOD),

**24** "you built yourself a vaulted chamber and made yourself a lofty place in every square.

**25** At the head of every street you built your lofty place and made your beauty an abomination, offering yourself to any passerby and multiplying your whoring.

**26** You played the prostitute with the Egyptians, your lustful neighbours, multiplying your whoring, to provoke me to anger.

**27** See, therefore, I stretched out my hand against you and diminished your allotted portion and delivered you to the greed of your enemies, the daughters of the Philistines, who were ashamed of your lewd behaviour.

**28** You played the prostitute also with the Assyrians, because you were not satisfied. Yes, you played the prostitute with them, and still you were not satisfied.

**29** You multiplied your whoring also with the trading land of Chaldea, and even with this you were not satisfied.

**30** How sick is your heart," declares the Lord GOD, "because you did all these things, the deeds of a brazen prostitute,

**31** building your vaulted chamber at the head of every street, and making your lofty place in every square. Yet you were not like a prostitute, because you scorned payment.

**32** Adulterous wife, who receives strangers instead of her husband.

**33** People give gifts to all prostitutes, but you gave your gifts to all your lovers, bribing them to come to you from every side for your whorings.

**34** So you were different from other women in your whorings. No one solicited you to play the prostitute, and you gave payment while no payment was given to you. Therefore you were different.

**35** Therefore, prostitute, hear the word of the LORD:

**36** Thus says the Lord GOD, Because your lust was poured out and your nakedness uncovered in your whorings with your lovers, and with all your abominable idols, and because of the blood of your children that you gave to them,

**37** therefore, see, I will gather all your lovers with whom you took pleasure, all those you loved and all those you hated. I will gather them against you from every side and will uncover your nakedness to them, that they may see all your nakedness.

**38** And I will judge you as women who commit adultery and shed blood are judged, and bring on you the blood of wrath and jealousy.

**39** And I will give you into the hand of your lovers, and they will throw down your vaulted chamber and break down your lofty places. They will strip you of your clothes and take your beautiful jewels and leave you naked and bare.

**40** They will bring up a crowd against you, and they will stone you and cut you to pieces with their swords.

**41** And they will burn your houses and execute judgements on you in the sight of many women. I will stop you from playing the prostitute, and you will give payment no more.

**42** So I will satisfy my fury on you, and my jealousy will turn away from you. I will be calm and will no more be angry.

**43** Because you have not remembered the days of your youth, but have enraged me with all these things, therefore, see, I have brought your deeds on your head," declares the Lord GOD. "Have you not committed lewdness in addition to all your abominations?

**44** See, everyone who uses proverbs will use this proverb about you: 'Like mother, like daughter.'

**45** You are the daughter of your mother, who loathed her husband and her children, and you are the sister of your sisters, who loathed their husbands and their children. Your mother was a Hittite and your father an Amorite.

**46** And your elder sister is Samaria, who lived with her daughters to the north of you, and your younger sister, who lived to the south of you, is Sodom with her daughters.

**47** Not only did you walk in their ways and do according to their abominations. Within a very little time you were more corrupt than they in all your ways.

**48** As I live," declares the Lord GOD, "your sister Sodom and her daughters have not done as you and your daughters have done.

**49** See, this was the guilt of your sister Sodom: she and her daughters had pride, excess of food and prosperous ease, but did not aid the poor and needy.

**50** They were haughty and did an abominable thing before me. So I removed them, when I saw it.

**51** Samaria has not committed half your sins. You have committed more abominations than they, and have made your sisters appear righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] by all the abominations that you have committed.

**52** Bear your disgrace, you also, for you have intervened on behalf of your sisters. Because of your sins in which you acted more abominably than they, they are more in the right \[H-TSEDAQAH\] than you. So be ashamed, you also, and bear your disgrace, for you have made your sisters appear righteous.

**53** I will restore their fortunes \[H-SHUV\], both the fortunes of Sodom and her daughters and the fortunes of Samaria and her daughters, and I will restore your own fortunes in the midst of them,

**54** that you may bear your disgrace and be ashamed of all that you have done, becoming a consolation \[H-NACHAM\] to them.

**55** As for your sisters, Sodom and her daughters will return \[H-SHUV\] to their former estate, and Samaria and her daughters will return \[H-SHUV\] to their former estate, and you and your daughters will return \[H-SHUV\] to your former estate.

**56** Was not your sister Sodom a byword in your mouth in the day of your pride,

**57** before your wickedness was uncovered? Now you have become an object of reproach for the daughters of Syria and all those around her, and for the daughters of the Philistines, those all around who despise you.

**58** You bear the penalty of your lewdness and your abominations," declares the LORD.

**59** "For thus says the Lord GOD: I will deal with you as you have done, you who have despised the oath in breaking the covenant \[H-BERIT\],

**60** yet I will remember my covenant \[H-BERIT\] with you in the days of your youth, and I will establish for you an everlasting covenant \[H-BERIT\].

**61** Then you will remember your ways and be ashamed when you take your sisters, your elder and your younger, and I give them to you as daughters, but not on account of the covenant \[H-BERIT\] with you.

**62** I will establish my covenant \[H-BERIT\] with you, and you will know that I am the LORD,

**63** that you may remember and be confounded, and never open your mouth again because of your shame, when I atone \[H-KAPPER\] for you for all that you have done," declares the Lord GOD.

### Chapter 17

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "Son of man, propound a riddle, and speak a parable to the house of Israel.

**3** Say, Thus says the Lord GOD: A great eagle with great wings and long pinions, rich in plumage of many colours, came to Lebanon and took the top of the cedar.

**4** He broke off the topmost of its young twigs and carried it to a land of trade and set it in a city of merchants.

**5** Then he took a seed of the land and planted it in fertile soil. He placed it beside abundant waters. He set it like a willow twig.

**6** And it sprouted and became a low spreading vine, with its branches turned toward him, and its roots remained where it stood. So it became a vine and produced branches and put out boughs.

**7** And there was another great eagle with great wings and much plumage. And see, this vine bent its roots toward him and shot out its branches toward him from the bed where it was planted, that he might water it.

**8** It had been planted on good soil by abundant waters, that it might produce branches and bear fruit and become a noble vine.

**9** Say, Thus says the Lord GOD: Will it thrive? Will he not pull up its roots and cut off its fruit, so that it withers, so that all its fresh sprouting leaves wither? It will not take a strong arm or many people to pull it from its roots.

**10** See, it is planted. Will it thrive? Will it not utterly wither when the east wind \[H-RUACH\] touches it, wither away on the bed where it grew?"

**11** Then the word of the LORD came to me:

**12** "Say now to the rebellious house, Do you not know what these things mean? Tell them, See, the king of Babylon came to Jerusalem, and took her king and her princes and brought them to him in Babylon.

**13** And he took one of the royal offspring and made a covenant \[H-BERIT\] with him, putting him under oath (the chief people of the land he had taken away),

**14** that the kingdom might be humble and not lift itself up, and keep his covenant \[H-BERIT\] that it might stand.

**15** But he rebelled against him by sending his ambassadors to Egypt, that they might give him horses and a large army. Will he thrive? Can one escape who does such things? Can he break the covenant \[H-BERIT\] and yet escape?

**16** As I live," declares the Lord GOD, "surely in the place where the king lives who made him king, whose oath he despised, and whose covenant \[H-BERIT\] with him he broke, in Babylon he will die.

**17** Pharaoh with his mighty army and great company will not help him in war, when ramps are cast up and siege walls built to cut off many lives \[H-NEPHESH\].

**18** He despised the oath in breaking the covenant \[H-BERIT\], and see, he gave his hand and did all these things. He will not escape.

**19** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: As I live, surely it is my oath that he has despised, and my covenant \[H-BERIT\] that he has broken. I will bring it on his head.

**20** I will spread my net over him, and he will be taken in my snare, and I will bring him to Babylon and enter into judgement with him there for the treachery he has committed against me.

**21** And all the pick of his troops will fall by the sword, and the survivors will be scattered to every wind \[H-RUACH\], and you will know that I, the LORD, have spoken."

**22** Thus says the Lord GOD: "I myself will take a sprig from the lofty top of the cedar and will set it out. I will break off from the topmost of its young twigs a tender one, and I myself will plant it on a high and lofty mountain.

**23** On the mountain height of Israel I will plant it, that it may bear branches and produce fruit and become a noble cedar. And under it will live every kind of bird. In the shade of its branches birds of every sort will nest.

**24** And all the trees of the field will know that I am the LORD. I bring low the high tree, and make high the low tree, dry up the green tree, and make the dry tree flourish. I, the LORD, have spoken, and I will do it."

### Chapter 18

**1** The word of the LORD came to me:

**2** "What do you mean by repeating this proverb concerning the land of Israel, 'The fathers have eaten sour grapes, and the children's teeth are set on edge'?

**3** As I live," declares the Lord GOD, "this proverb will no more be used by you in Israel.

**4** See, all souls \[H-NEPHESH\] are mine. The soul \[H-NEPHESH\] of the father as well as the soul \[H-NEPHESH\] of the son is mine. The soul \[H-NEPHESH\] who sins will die.

**5** If a man is righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] and does what is just and right,

**6** if he does not eat on the mountains or lift up his eyes to the idols of the house of Israel, does not defile his neighbour's wife or approach a woman in her time of uncleanness,

**7** does not oppress anyone, but restores to the debtor his pledge, commits no robbery, gives his bread to the hungry and covers the naked with a garment,

**8** does not lend at interest or take any profit, withholds his hand from injustice, executes true justice between one person and another,

**9** walks in my statutes and keeps my rules by acting faithfully, he is righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\]. He will surely live," declares the Lord GOD.

**10** "If he fathers a son who is violent, a shedder of blood, who does any of these things

**11** (though he himself did none of these things), who even eats on the mountains, defiles his neighbour's wife,

**12** oppresses the poor and needy, commits robbery, does not restore the pledge, lifts up his eyes to the idols, commits abomination,

**13** lends at interest and takes profit, will he then live? He will not live. He has done all these abominations. He will surely die. His blood will be on himself.

**14** Now suppose this man fathers a son who sees all the sins that his father has done. He sees, and does not do likewise.

**15** He does not eat on the mountains or lift up his eyes to the idols of the house of Israel, does not defile his neighbour's wife,

**16** does not oppress anyone, exacts no pledge, commits no robbery, but gives his bread to the hungry and covers the naked with a garment,

**17** withholds his hand from iniquity, takes no interest or profit, keeps my rules and walks in my statutes. He will not die for his father's iniquity. He will surely live.

**18** As for his father, because he practised extortion, robbed his brother and did what is not good among his people, see, he will die for his iniquity.

**19** Yet you say, 'Why should not the son suffer for the iniquity of the father?' When the son has done what is just and right, and has been careful to keep all my statutes, he will surely live.

**20** The soul \[H-NEPHESH\] who sins will die. The son will not suffer for the iniquity of the father, nor the father suffer for the iniquity of the son. The righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] of the righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] will be on himself, and the wickedness of the wicked will be on himself.

**21** But if the wicked person turns \[H-SHUV\] away from all his sins that he has committed and keeps all my statutes and does what is just and right, he will surely live. He will not die.

**22** None of the transgressions that he has committed will be remembered against him. For the righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] that he has done he will live.

**23** Have I any pleasure in the death of the wicked," declares the Lord GOD, "and not rather that he should turn \[H-SHUV\] from his way and live?

**24** But when a righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] person turns \[H-SHUV\] away from his righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] and does injustice and does the same abominations that the wicked person does, will he live? None of the righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] deeds that he has done will be remembered. For the treachery of which he is guilty and the sin he has committed, for them he will die.

**25** Yet you say, 'The way of the Lord is not just.' Hear now, house of Israel: Is my way not just? Is it not your ways that are not just?

**26** When a righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\] person turns \[H-SHUV\] away from his righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] and does injustice, he will die for it. For the injustice that he has done he will die.

**27** Again, when a wicked person turns \[H-SHUV\] away from the wickedness he has committed and does what is just and right, he will save his life \[H-NEPHESH\].

**28** Because he considered and turned \[H-SHUV\] away from all the transgressions that he had committed, he will surely live. He will not die.

**29** Yet the house of Israel says, 'The way of the Lord is not just.' House of Israel, are my ways not just? Is it not your ways that are not just?

**30** Therefore I will judge you, house of Israel, every one according to his ways," declares the Lord GOD. "Return \[H-SHUV\] and turn \[H-SHUV\] away from all your transgressions, lest iniquity be your ruin.

**31** Cast away from you all the transgressions that you have committed, and make yourselves a new heart and a new spirit \[H-RUACH\]. Why will you die, house of Israel?

**32** For I have no pleasure in the death of anyone," declares the Lord GOD. "So turn \[H-SHUV\], and live."

### Chapter 19

**1** "And you, take up a lamentation for the princes of Israel,

**2** and say: What was your mother? A lioness. Among lions she crouched. In the midst of young lions she reared her cubs.

**3** And she brought up one of her cubs. He became a young lion, and he learned to catch prey. He devoured people.

**4** The nations heard about him. He was caught in their pit, and they brought him with hooks to the land of Egypt.

**5** When she saw that she waited in vain, that her hope was lost, she took another of her cubs and made him a young lion.

**6** He prowled among the lions. He became a young lion, and he learned to catch prey. He devoured people,

**7** and ravaged their strongholds and laid waste their cities. And the land was appalled, and all who were in it, at the sound of his roaring.

**8** Then the nations set against him from provinces on every side. They spread their net over him. He was taken in their pit.

**9** With hooks they put him in a cage and brought him to the king of Babylon. They brought him into custody, that his voice should no more be heard on the mountains of Israel.

**10** Your mother was like a vine in a vineyard planted by the water, fruitful and full of branches by reason of abundant water.

**11** Its strong stems became rulers' sceptres. It towered aloft among the thick boughs. It was seen in its height with the mass of its branches.

**12** But the vine was plucked up in fury, cast down to the ground. The east wind \[H-RUACH\] dried up its fruit. Its strong stem was torn off and withered. The fire consumed it.

**13** Now it is planted in the wilderness, in a dry and thirsty land.

**14** And fire has gone out from its stem, has consumed its shoots and fruit, so that there remains in it no strong stem, no sceptre for ruling." This is a lamentation and has become a lamentation.

*Review note: Ezekiel 12 to 19 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the sign of the exile's baggage and the word on the prince (12:1-16), the bread eaten in trembling (12:17-20), the proverb that is ended (12:21-28), the false prophets and the wall smeared with whitewash (13:1-16), the women who hunt souls (13:17-23), the idols in the heart and the three men Noah, Daniel and Job (14:1-23), the vine that is burned (15:1-8), the foundling who becomes the unfaithful wife and the everlasting covenant (16:1-63), the two eagles and the vine, and the sprig of the cedar (17:1-24), the proverb of the sour grapes and the one who sins (18:1-32), and the lament for the princes (19:1-14). The chapters are in prose, with the poetry of the lament in chapter 19 and parts of chapters 15 and 17. In 12:14, 13:3, 13:11, 13:13, 17:10, 17:21, 18:31 and 19:12 ruach is tagged H-RUACH, with the capital letter not used in these places. In 13:10 and 13:16 "peace" is tagged H-SHALOM. In 13:18, 13:19 and 13:20 "souls" translates nefashot and is tagged H-NEPHESH each time, and in 13:22 "to keep him alive" is lehachayyoto, and is not tagged. In 14:14, 14:20, 17:17, 18:4, 18:20 and 18:27 "life", "lives" and "soul" translate nefesh and are tagged H-NEPHESH. In 13:22, 14:14, 14:20, 16:51, 16:52 and in chapter 18 "righteous" and "righteousness" are tagged H-TSEDAQAH each time. In 14:6, 16:53, 16:55 and in chapter 18 forms of shuv are tagged H-SHUV, and in 16:53 the idiom is "restore the fortunes" (shuv shevut). In 14:22 and 14:23 and 16:54 forms of nacham are tagged H-NACHAM. In 16:8, 16:59, 16:60, 16:61, 16:62 and in chapter 17 "covenant" is tagged H-BERIT. In 16:63 "when I atone for you" translates be-khapperi lakh and is tagged H-KAPPER. In 18:2 the proverb of the sour grapes is as in Jeremiah 31:29, and the wording is matched. In 18:31 "a new heart and a new spirit" is as in 11:19 and 36:26, and the footnote records the difference of 11:19 ("one heart"). The words on the children sacrificed (16:20-21) and on the sins of Sodom (16:49-50) are rendered as written, with no comment. In 16:57 "Syria" is given as the Hebrew has it, and many manuscripts read "Edom", and the footnote records the matter. In 14:14 and 14:20 "Daniel" translates Dani'el, and the footnote records the matter. There are no departures from the Hebrew in chapters 12 to 19. The Hebrew is hard in some places, including 12:13, 13:2, 13:18-20, 14:9, 16:30, 16:36, 17:9, 17:17, 18:7 and 19:5-9. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Five: The History of Rebellion and the Sword of the LORD (Chapters 20 to 24)

### Chapter 20

**1** In the seventh year, in the fifth month, on the tenth day of the month, certain of the elders of Israel came to consult the LORD, and sat before me.

**2** And the word of the LORD came to me:

**3** "Son of man, speak to the elders of Israel, and say to them, Thus says the Lord GOD: Is it to consult me that you have come? As I live," declares the Lord GOD, "I will not be consulted by you.

**4** Will you judge them, son of man? Will you judge them? Make known to them the abominations of their fathers,

**5** and say to them, Thus says the Lord GOD: On the day when I chose Israel, I swore to the offspring of the house of Jacob (making myself known to them in the land of Egypt), I swore to them, saying, I am the LORD your God.

**6** On that day I swore to them that I would bring them out of the land of Egypt into a land that I had searched out for them, a land flowing with milk and honey, the glory of all lands.

**7** And I said to them, Cast away the detestable things your eyes feast on, every one of you, and do not defile yourselves with the idols of Egypt. I am the LORD your God.
