**11** with a passage before them. They were like the chambers on the north, of the same length and breadth, with the same exits and arrangements and doors.

**12** And as were the doors of the chambers on the south, so was the door at the opening of the passage, the passage in front of the corresponding wall toward the east, as one enters them.

**13** Then he said to me, "The north chambers and the south chambers opposite the yard are the holy \[H-QADOSH\] chambers, where the priests who approach the LORD will eat the most holy \[H-QADOSH\] offerings. There they will put the most holy \[H-QADOSH\] offerings, the grain offering, the sin offering and the guilt offering, for the place is holy \[H-QADOSH\].

**14** When the priests enter the holy \[H-QADOSH\] place, they will not go out of it into the outer court without laying there the garments in which they minister, for these are holy \[H-QADOSH\]. They will put on other garments before they go near to that which is for the people."

**15** Now when he had finished measuring the interior of the temple area, he led me out by the gate that faces east, and measured the temple area all around.

**16** He measured the east side with the measuring reed, five hundred reeds by the measuring reed all around.

**17** He measured the north side, five hundred reeds by the measuring reed all around.

**18** He measured the south side, five hundred reeds by the measuring reed.

**19** Then he turned to the west side and measured five hundred reeds by the measuring reed.

**20** He measured it on the four sides. It had a wall around it, five hundred long and five hundred broad, to make a separation between the holy \[H-QADOSH\] and the common.

### Chapter 43

**1** Then he led me to the gate, the gate facing east.

**2** And see, the glory of the God of Israel came from the east. And the sound of his coming was like the sound of many waters, and the earth shone with his glory.

**3** And the vision I saw was like the vision that I had seen when I came to destroy the city, and like the vision that I had seen by the river Chebar. And I fell on my face.

**4** As the glory of the LORD entered the temple by the gate facing east,

**5** the spirit \[H-RUACH\] lifted me up and brought me into the inner court. And see, the glory of the LORD filled the temple.

**6** While the man was standing beside me, I heard one speaking to me out of the temple,

**7** and he said to me, "Son of man, this is the place of my throne and the place of the soles of my feet, where I will live in the midst of the people of Israel for ever. And the house of Israel will no more defile my holy \[H-QADOSH\] name, neither they nor their kings, by their whoring and by the dead bodies of their kings at their high places,

**8** by setting their threshold by my threshold and their doorposts beside my doorposts, with only a wall between me and them. They have defiled my holy \[H-QADOSH\] name by their abominations that they have done, so I have consumed them in my anger.

**9** Now let them put away their whoring and the dead bodies of their kings far from me, and I will live in their midst for ever.

**10** As for you, son of man, describe to the house of Israel the temple, and let them measure its plan, that they may be ashamed of their iniquities.

**11** And if they are ashamed of all that they have done, make known to them the design of the temple, its arrangement, its exits and its entrances, that is, its whole design, and all its statutes and all its teachings \[H-TORAH\]. And write it down in their sight, so that they may keep all its teachings and all its statutes and carry them out.

**12** This is the teaching \[H-TORAH\] of the temple: the whole territory on the top of the mountain all around will be most holy \[H-QADOSH\]. See, this is the teaching \[H-TORAH\] of the temple.

**13** These are the measurements of the altar by cubits (the cubit being a cubit and a handbreadth): its base will be a cubit high and a cubit broad, with a rim of one span around its edge. And this will be the height of the altar:

**14** from the base on the ground to the lower ledge, two cubits, with a breadth of one cubit, and from the smaller ledge to the larger ledge, four cubits, with a breadth of one cubit.

**15** And the altar hearth is four cubits, and from the altar hearth projecting upward are four horns.

**16** The altar hearth will be square, twelve cubits long by twelve broad.

**17** The ledge also will be square, fourteen cubits long by fourteen broad, with a rim around it half a cubit broad, and its base will be a cubit all around. Its steps will face east."

**18** And he said to me, "Son of man, thus says the Lord GOD: These are the ordinances for the altar. On the day when it is erected for offering burnt offerings on it and for sprinkling blood on it,

**19** you will give to the Levitical priests of the family of Zadok, who draw near to me to minister to me," declares the Lord GOD, "a bull from the herd for a sin offering.

**20** And you will take some of its blood and put it on the four horns of the altar and on the four corners of the ledge and on the rim all around. Thus you will purify it and make atonement \[H-KAPPER\] for it.

**21** You will also take the bull of the sin offering, and it will be burned in the appointed place belonging to the temple, outside the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\].

**22** And on the second day you will offer a male goat without blemish for a sin offering, and the altar will be purified, as it was purified with the bull.

**23** When you have finished purifying it, you will offer a bull from the herd without blemish and a ram from the flock without blemish.

**24** You will present them before the LORD, and the priests will sprinkle salt on them and offer them up as a burnt offering to the LORD.

**25** For seven days you will provide daily a male goat for a sin offering. Also a bull from the herd and a ram from the flock, without blemish, will be provided.

**26** Seven days they will make atonement \[H-KAPPER\] for the altar and cleanse it, and so consecrate it.

**27** And when they have completed these days, then from the eighth day onward the priests will offer on the altar your burnt offerings and your peace offerings, and I will accept you," declares the Lord GOD.

### Chapter 44

**1** Then he brought me back to the outer gate of the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\], which faces east, and it was shut.

**2** And the LORD said to me, "This gate will remain shut. It will not be opened, and no one will enter by it, for the LORD, the God of Israel, has entered by it. Therefore it will remain shut.

**3** Only the prince may sit in it to eat bread before the LORD. He will enter by way of the vestibule of the gate, and will go out by the same way."

**4** Then he brought me by way of the north gate to the front of the temple, and I looked, and see, the glory of the LORD filled the temple of the LORD. And I fell on my face.

**5** And the LORD said to me, "Son of man, mark well, see with your eyes, and listen with your ears to all that I will tell you concerning all the ordinances of the temple of the LORD and all its teachings \[H-TORAH\]. And mark well the entrance to the temple and all the exits of the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\].

**6** And say to the rebellious house, to the house of Israel, Thus says the Lord GOD: House of Israel, let there be an end to all your abominations,

**7** in that you have brought in foreigners, uncircumcised in heart and flesh, to be in my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\], profaning my house, when you offer to me my food, the fat and the blood. You have broken my covenant \[H-BERIT\], in addition to all your abominations.

**8** And you have not kept charge of my holy things \[H-QADOSH\], but you have set others to keep my charge in my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\].

**9** Thus says the Lord GOD: No foreigner, uncircumcised in heart and flesh, of all the foreigners who are among the people of Israel, will enter my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\].

**10** But the Levites who went far from me, going astray from me after their idols when Israel went astray, will bear their punishment.

**11** They will be ministers in my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\], having oversight at the gates of the temple and ministering in the temple. They will slaughter the burnt offering and the sacrifices for the people, and they will stand before the people to minister to them.

**12** Because they ministered to them before their idols and became a stumbling block of iniquity to the house of Israel, therefore I have sworn concerning them," declares the Lord GOD, "and they will bear their punishment.

**13** They will not come near to me, to serve me as priest, nor come near any of my holy things \[H-QADOSH\] and the things that are most holy \[H-QADOSH\], but they will bear their shame and the abominations that they have done.

**14** Yet I will appoint them to keep charge of the temple, to do all its service and all that is to be done in it.

**15** But the Levitical priests, the sons of Zadok, who kept the charge of my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\] when the people of Israel went astray from me, will come near to me to minister to me, and they will stand before me to offer me the fat and the blood," declares the Lord GOD.

**16** "They will enter my sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\], and they will approach my table to minister to me, and they will keep my charge.

**17** When they enter the gates of the inner court, they will wear linen garments. They will have nothing of wool on them, while they minister at the gates of the inner court, and within.

**18** They will have linen caps on their heads, and linen undergarments around their waists. They will not gird themselves with anything that causes sweat.

**19** And when they go out to the outer court, to the people, they will put off the garments in which they have been ministering and lay them in the holy \[H-QADOSH\] chambers. And they will put on other garments, lest they communicate holiness \[H-QADOSH\] to the people with their garments.

**20** They will not shave their heads or let their locks grow long. They will only trim the hair of their heads.

**21** No priest will drink wine when he enters the inner court.

**22** They will not marry a widow or a divorced woman, but only virgins of the offspring of the house of Israel, or a widow who is the widow of a priest.

**23** They will teach my people the difference between the holy \[H-QADOSH\] and the common, and show them how to distinguish between the unclean and the clean.

**24** In a controversy, they will act as judges, and they will judge it according to my judgements. They will keep my teachings \[H-TORAH\] and my statutes in all my appointed feasts, and they will keep my Sabbaths holy \[H-QADOSH\].

**25** They will not defile themselves by going near to a dead person. However, for father or mother, for son or daughter, for brother or unmarried sister they may defile themselves.

**26** After he has become clean, they will count for him seven days.

**27** And on the day that he goes into the holy place, into the inner court, to minister in the holy place \[H-QADOSH\], he will offer his sin offering," declares the Lord GOD.

**28** "This will be their inheritance: I am their inheritance. And you will give them no possession in Israel. I am their possession.

**29** They will eat the grain offering, the sin offering and the guilt offering, and every devoted thing in Israel will be theirs.

**30** And the first of all the firstfruits of all kinds and every offering of all kinds from all your contributions will belong to the priests. You will also give to the priests the first of your dough, that a blessing may rest on your house.

**31** The priests will not eat any bird or animal, whether a carcass or torn by beasts.

### Chapter 45

**1** When you allot the land as an inheritance, you will set apart for the LORD a holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion of the land, twenty-five thousand cubits long and twenty thousand broad. It will be holy \[H-QADOSH\] throughout its whole extent.

**2** Of this a square plot of five hundred by five hundred cubits will be for the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\], with fifty cubits for an open space around it.

**3** And from this measured area you will measure off a section twenty-five thousand cubits long and ten thousand broad, in which will be the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\], the Most Holy Place \[H-QADOSH\].

**4** It will be a holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion of the land. It will be for the priests, who minister in the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\] and approach to minister to the LORD, and it will be a place for their houses and a holy place \[H-QADOSH\] for the sanctuary.

**5** Another section, twenty-five thousand cubits long and ten thousand broad, will be for the Levites who minister at the temple, as their possession, twenty chambers.

**6** Alongside the portion set apart as the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion you will assign for the possession of the city an area five thousand cubits broad and twenty-five thousand long. It will belong to the whole house of Israel.

**7** And to the prince will belong the land on both sides of the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion and the property of the city, alongside the holy portion and the property of the city, on the west and on the east, corresponding in length to one of the tribal portions, from the west boundary to the east boundary.

**8** It is to be his property in Israel. And my princes will no more oppress my people, but they will let the house of Israel have the land according to their tribes.

**9** Thus says the Lord GOD: Enough, princes of Israel. Put away violence and oppression, and execute justice and righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\]. Cease your evictions of my people," declares the Lord GOD.

**10** "You will have just balances \[H-TSEDAQAH\], a just ephah and a just bath.

**11** The ephah and the bath will be of the same measure, the bath containing a tenth of a homer, and the ephah a tenth of a homer. The homer will be the standard measure.

**12** The shekel will be twenty gerahs. Twenty shekels plus twenty-five shekels plus fifteen shekels will be your mina.

**13** This is the contribution that you will make: a sixth of an ephah from each homer of wheat, and a sixth of an ephah from each homer of barley,

**14** and the prescribed portion of oil, measured in baths, a tenth of a bath from each cor (which is ten baths or a homer, for ten baths make a homer),

**15** and one sheep from every flock of two hundred, from the pastures of Israel, for grain offering, burnt offering and peace offerings, to make atonement \[H-KAPPER\] for them," declares the Lord GOD.

**16** "All the people of the land will give this contribution to the prince in Israel.

**17** It will be the prince's duty to furnish the burnt offerings, grain offerings and drink offerings, at the feasts, the new moons and the Sabbaths, all the appointed feasts of the house of Israel. He will provide the sin offerings, grain offerings, burnt offerings and peace offerings, to make atonement \[H-KAPPER\] on behalf of the house of Israel.

**18** Thus says the Lord GOD: In the first month, on the first day of the month, you will take a bull from the herd without blemish, and purify the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\].

**19** The priest will take some of the blood of the sin offering and put it on the doorposts of the temple, the four corners of the ledge of the altar, and the posts of the gate of the inner court.

**20** You will do the same on the seventh day of the month for anyone who has sinned through error or ignorance. So you will make atonement \[H-KAPPER\] for the temple.

**21** In the first month, on the fourteenth day of the month, you will celebrate the Feast of the Passover, and for seven days unleavened bread will be eaten.

**22** On that day the prince will provide for himself and all the people of the land a bull for a sin offering.

**23** And on the seven days of the festival he will provide as a burnt offering to the LORD seven bulls and seven rams without blemish, on each of the seven days, and a male goat daily for a sin offering.

**24** And he will provide as a grain offering an ephah for each bull, an ephah for each ram, and a hin of oil to an ephah.

**25** In the seventh month, on the fifteenth day of the month and for the seven days of the feast, he will make the same provision for sin offerings, burnt offerings and grain offerings, and for the oil."

### Chapter 46

**1** Thus says the Lord GOD: The gate of the inner court that faces east will be shut on the six working days, but on the Sabbath it will be opened, and on the day of the new moon it will be opened.

**2** The prince will enter by the exterior of the vestibule of the gate, and will take his stand by the doorpost of the gate. The priests will offer his burnt offering and his peace offerings, and he will bow down at the threshold of the gate. Then he will go out, but the gate will not be shut until evening.

**3** The people of the land will bow down at the entrance of that gate before the LORD on the Sabbaths and on the new moons.

**4** The burnt offering that the prince offers to the LORD on the Sabbath day will be six lambs without blemish and a ram without blemish.

**5** And the grain offering with the ram will be an ephah, and the grain offering with the lambs as much as he is able to give, together with a hin of oil to an ephah.

**6** On the day of the new moon he will offer a bull from the herd without blemish, and six lambs and a ram. They will be without blemish.

**7** As a grain offering he will provide an ephah with the bull and an ephah with the ram, and with the lambs as much as he is able, together with a hin of oil to an ephah.

**8** When the prince enters, he will enter by the vestibule of the gate, and he will go out by the same way.

**9** When the people of the land come before the LORD at the appointed feasts, the one who enters by the north gate to bow down will go out by the south gate, and the one who enters by the south gate will go out by the north gate. No one will return by the way by which he entered, but each will go out straight ahead.

**10** When they enter, the prince will enter with them, and when they go out, he will go out.

**11** At the feasts and the appointed festivals, the grain offering with a bull will be an ephah, and with a ram an ephah, and with the lambs as much as one is able to give, together with a hin of oil to an ephah.

**12** When the prince provides a freewill offering, either a burnt offering or peace offerings as a freewill offering to the LORD, the gate facing east will be opened for him. And he will offer his burnt offering or his peace offerings as he does on the Sabbath day. Then he will go out, and after he has gone out the gate will be shut.

**13** You will provide a lamb a year old without blemish as a burnt offering to the LORD daily. Morning by morning you will provide it.

**14** And you will provide a grain offering with it morning by morning, a sixth of an ephah, and a third of a hin of oil to moisten the flour, as a grain offering to the LORD. This is a perpetual statute.

**15** Thus they will provide the lamb and the grain offering and the oil, morning by morning, as a regular burnt offering.

**16** Thus says the Lord GOD: If the prince makes a gift to any of his sons as his inheritance, it will belong to his sons. It is their holding by inheritance.

**17** But if he makes a gift out of his inheritance to one of his servants, it will be his to the year of liberty. Then it will revert to the prince. Only his sons may keep a gift from his inheritance.

**18** The prince will not take any of the inheritance of the people, thrusting them out of their property. He will give his sons their inheritance out of his own property, so that none of my people will be dispersed from his property.

**19** Then he brought me through the entrance, which was at the side of the gate, to the north row of the holy \[H-QADOSH\] chambers for the priests, and see, a place was there at the extreme western end of them.

**20** And he said to me, "This is the place where the priests will boil the guilt offering and the sin offering, and where they will bake the grain offering, in order not to bring them out into the outer court and so communicate holiness \[H-QADOSH\] to the people."

**21** Then he brought me out to the outer court and led me around the four corners of the court. And see, in each corner of the court there was another court.

**22** In the four corners of the court were small courts, forty cubits long and thirty broad. The four were of the same size.

**23** On the inside, around each of the four courts was a row of masonry, with hearths made at the bottom of the rows all around.

**24** Then he said to me, "These are the kitchens where those who minister at the temple will boil the sacrifices of the people."

### Chapter 47

**1** Then he brought me back to the door of the temple, and see, water was issuing from below the threshold of the temple toward the east (for the temple faced east). The water was flowing down from below the south end of the threshold of the temple, south of the altar.

**2** Then he brought me out by way of the north gate and led me around on the outside to the outer gate that faces toward the east. And see, the water was trickling out on the south side.

**3** Going on eastward with a measuring line in his hand, the man measured a thousand cubits, and then led me through the water, and it was ankle-deep.

**4** Again he measured a thousand, and led me through the water, and it was knee-deep. Again he measured a thousand, and led me through the water, and it was waist-deep.

**5** Again he measured a thousand, and it was a river that I could not pass through, for the water had risen. It was deep enough to swim in, a river that could not be passed through.

**6** And he said to me, "Son of man, have you seen this?" Then he led me back to the bank of the river.

**7** As I went back, see, on the bank of the river were very many trees on the one side and on the other.

**8** And he said to me, "This water flows toward the eastern region and goes down into the Arabah, and enters the sea. When the water flows into the sea, the water of the sea will become fresh.

**9** And wherever the river goes, every living creature that swarms will live, and there will be very many fish. For this water goes there, that the waters of the sea may become fresh. So everything will live where the river goes.

**10** Fishers will stand beside the sea. From En-gedi to En-eglaim it will be a place for the spreading of nets. Its fish will be of very many kinds, like the fish of the Great Sea.

**11** But its swamps and marshes will not become fresh. They are to be left for salt.

**12** And on the banks, on both sides of the river, there will grow all kinds of trees for food. Their leaves will not wither, nor their fruit fail, but they will bear fresh fruit every month, because their water flows from the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\]. Their fruit will be for food, and their leaves for healing."

**13** Thus says the Lord GOD: "These are the boundaries by which you will divide the land for inheritance among the twelve tribes of Israel. Joseph will have two portions.

**14** And you will divide it equally, one as much as another. I swore to give it to your fathers, and this land will fall to you as your inheritance.

**15** This will be the boundary of the land: On the north side, from the Great Sea by way of Hethlon to Lebo-hamath, and on to Zedad,

**16** Berothah, Sibraim (which lies on the boundary between Damascus and Hamath), as far as Hazer-hatticon, which is on the boundary of Hauran.

**17** So the boundary will run from the sea to Hazar-enan, which is on the northern boundary of Damascus, with the boundary of Hamath to the north. This will be the north side.

**18** On the east side, the boundary will run between Hauran and Damascus. Between Gilead and the land of Israel will be the Jordan. From the northern boundary to the eastern sea you will measure. This will be the east side.

**19** On the south side, it will run from Tamar as far as the waters of Meribath-kadesh, then along the Brook of Egypt to the Great Sea. This will be the south side.

**20** On the west side, the Great Sea will be the boundary to a point opposite Lebo-hamath. This will be the west side.

**21** So you will divide this land among you according to the tribes of Israel.

**22** You will allot it as an inheritance for yourselves and for the foreigners who live among you and who have fathered children among you. They will be to you as native-born children of Israel. With you they will be allotted an inheritance among the tribes of Israel.

**23** In whatever tribe the foreigner lives, there you will assign him his inheritance," declares the Lord GOD.

### Chapter 48

**1** These are the names of the tribes: From the northern border, beside the way of Hethlon to Lebo-hamath, as far as Hazar-enan (which is on the northern border of Damascus, with Hamath to the north), and extending from the east side to the west, Dan, one portion.

**2** Adjoining the territory of Dan, from the east side to the west, Asher, one portion.

**3** Adjoining the territory of Asher, from the east side to the west, Naphtali, one portion.

**4** Adjoining the territory of Naphtali, from the east side to the west, Manasseh, one portion.

**5** Adjoining the territory of Manasseh, from the east side to the west, Ephraim, one portion.

**6** Adjoining the territory of Ephraim, from the east side to the west, Reuben, one portion.

**7** Adjoining the territory of Reuben, from the east side to the west, Judah, one portion.

**8** Adjoining the territory of Judah, from the east side to the west, will be the portion that you will set apart, twenty-five thousand cubits in breadth, and in length equal to one of the tribal portions, from the east side to the west, with the sanctuary \[H-QADOSH\] in the midst of it.

**9** The portion that you will set apart for the LORD will be twenty-five thousand cubits in length, and ten thousand in breadth.

**10** These will be the allotments of the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion: for the priests, to the north twenty-five thousand cubits in length, to the west ten thousand in breadth, to the east ten thousand in breadth, and to the south twenty-five thousand in length, with the sanctuary of the LORD in the midst of it.

**11** This will be for the consecrated \[H-QADOSH\] priests, the sons of Zadok, who kept my charge, who did not go astray when the people of Israel went astray, as the Levites did.

**12** And it will belong to them as a special portion from the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion of the land, a most holy \[H-QADOSH\] place, adjoining the territory of the Levites.

**13** And alongside the territory of the priests, the Levites will have an allotment twenty-five thousand cubits in length and ten thousand in breadth. The whole length will be twenty-five thousand cubits and the breadth ten thousand.

**14** They will not sell or exchange any of it. They will not alienate this best of the land, for it is holy \[H-QADOSH\] to the LORD.

**15** The remaining area, five thousand cubits in breadth and twenty-five thousand in length, will be for common use for the city, for dwellings and for open country. In the midst of it will be the city.

**16** And these will be its measurements: the north side four thousand five hundred cubits, the south side four thousand five hundred, the east side four thousand five hundred, and the west side four thousand five hundred.

**17** And the city will have open land: on the north two hundred and fifty cubits, on the south two hundred and fifty, on the east two hundred and fifty, on the west two hundred and fifty.

**18** The remainder of the length alongside the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion will be ten thousand cubits to the east, and ten thousand to the west, and it will be alongside the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion. Its produce will be food for the workers of the city.

**19** And the workers of the city, from all the tribes of Israel, will cultivate it.

**20** The whole portion that you will set apart will be twenty-five thousand cubits square, that is, the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion together with the property of the city.

**21** What remains on both sides of the holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion and of the property of the city will belong to the prince. Extending from the twenty-five thousand cubits of the holy portion to the east border, and westward from the twenty-five thousand to the west border, parallel to the tribal portions, it will belong to the prince. The holy \[H-QADOSH\] portion with the sanctuary of the temple in the middle of it,

**22** and the property of the Levites and the property of the city, will be in the middle of that which belongs to the prince. The portion of the prince will lie between the territory of Judah and the territory of Benjamin.

**23** As for the rest of the tribes: from the east side to the west, Benjamin, one portion.

**24** Adjoining the territory of Benjamin, from the east side to the west, Simeon, one portion.

**25** Adjoining the territory of Simeon, from the east side to the west, Issachar, one portion.

**26** Adjoining the territory of Issachar, from the east side to the west, Zebulun, one portion.

**27** Adjoining the territory of Zebulun, from the east side to the west, Gad, one portion.

**28** And adjoining the territory of Gad to the south, the boundary will run from Tamar to the waters of Meribath-kadesh, along the Brook of Egypt to the Great Sea.

**29** This is the land that you will allot as an inheritance among the tribes of Israel, and these are their portions," declares the Lord GOD.

**30** "These will be the exits of the city: On the north side, which is to be four thousand five hundred cubits by measure,

**31** three gates, the gate of Reuben, the gate of Judah and the gate of Levi, the gates of the city being named after the tribes of Israel.

**32** On the east side, which is four thousand five hundred cubits, three gates, the gate of Joseph, the gate of Benjamin and the gate of Dan.

**33** On the south side, which is to be four thousand five hundred cubits by measure, three gates, the gate of Simeon, the gate of Issachar and the gate of Zebulun.

**34** On the west side, which is four thousand five hundred cubits, three gates, the gate of Gad, the gate of Asher and the gate of Naphtali.

**35** The circumference of the city will be eighteen thousand cubits. And the name of the city from that time on will be, The LORD Is There."

*Review note: Ezekiel 40 to 48 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the vision of the new temple and the land: the man with the measuring reed, the gates and the courts (40:1-49), the temple, the nave, the Most Holy Place and the carvings (41:1-26), the chambers of the priests and the outer wall (42:1-20), the return of the glory of the LORD by the east gate and the altar (43:1-27), the closed gate, the priests and the Levites (44:1-31), the holy portion, the prince and the offerings (45:1-25), the offerings of the prince and the kitchens (46:1-24), the river from the temple and the boundaries of the land (47:1-23) and the portions of the tribes, the city and its name (48:1-35). The vision is dated in 40:1 to the twenty-fifth year of the exile, at the beginning of the year, on the tenth day, which is April 573 before Christ. The chapters are in prose, and the text is full of measures and architectural words, and the footnotes record the terms that are rare or not known. In 41:4, 41:21, 42:13, 42:14, 42:20, 43:7, 43:8, 43:12, 43:21, 44:1, 44:5, 44:7-9, 44:11, 44:13, 44:15, 44:16, 44:19, 44:23, 44:24, 44:27, 45:1-7, 45:18, 46:19, 46:20, 47:12 and in 48:8-21 "holy", "sanctuary", "Most Holy Place" and "consecrated" are tagged H-QADOSH. In 43:20, 43:26, 45:15, 45:17 and 45:20 "make atonement" translates forms of kipper and is tagged H-KAPPER. In 43:11, 43:12, 44:5 and 44:24 "teaching" and "teachings" translate torah and torot and are tagged H-TORAH. In 44:7 "covenant" is tagged H-BERIT. In 45:9 and 45:10 "righteousness" and "just" translate tsedaqah and tsedeq and are tagged H-TSEDAQAH. In 43:5 "the spirit" is tagged H-RUACH. In 43:27 and 45:15 "peace offerings" translates shelamim and is not tagged. In 42:16-19 "reeds" is given as the Hebrew has it, and the Greek has "cubits", and verses 42:20 and 45:2 give the measure without the word "reeds" or in cubits, and the footnote records the matter. In 43:7 "at their high places" follows the reading tradition (bamotam), and the written text has "in their death" (bemotam), and the footnote records the matter. In 48:35 the name "The LORD Is There" translates YHWH shammah. The words on the offerings, the priests and the portions are rendered as written, with no comment on how they are read. There are no departures from the Hebrew in chapters 40 to 48. The Hebrew is very hard in some places, including 40:5-16, 40:43, 41:6-12, 41:16-17, 42:3-12, 43:13-17, 45:1-8, 46:19-24 and 47:5. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Appendix A: Footnotes

- **1:1** "The thirtieth year" is not explained in the Hebrew, and the verse is hard. "The river Chebar" translates *nehar-Kevar*, a canal in Babylonia. "The heavens were opened" translates *nifthechu ha-shamayim*, and "visions of God" translates *mar'ot Elohim*, as in 8:3 and 40:2.
- **1:2** "The fifth year of the exile of King Jehoiachin" is 593 before Christ, and "the fifth day of the month" in the fourth month is July of that year.
- **1:3** "Ezekiel" translates *Yechezqe'l*, "God strengthens". "The hand of the LORD" translates *yad-YHWH*.
- **1:4** "A stormy wind" translates *ruach se'arah*. See entry H-RUACH. "Flashing continually" translates *mitlaqqachat*, and "gleaming metal" translates *chashmal*, a rare word that is not known.
- **1:5** "Living creatures" translates *chayyot*. They are called cherubim in 10:15 and 10:20.
- **1:7** "Burnished" translates *qalal*.
- **1:10** "A human face" translates *penei adam*.
- **1:13** "Burning coals of fire" translates *gachalei-esh*, and "torches" translates *lappidim*.
- **1:14** "Darted back and forth" translates *ratso va-shov*, in which the second verb is *shuv*. The text is not tagged.
- **1:15** "A wheel" translates *ofan*.
- **1:16** "Beryl" translates *tarshish*, and "one wheel within another" translates *ofan betokh ha-ofan*.
- **1:18** "Their rims" translates *gabbeihem*, and "awesome" translates *yir'ah*.
- **1:20** "The spirit of the living creature" translates *ruach ha-chayyah*, in which the noun is singular. See entry H-RUACH.
- **1:22** "A dome" translates *raqia*, the word of Genesis 1:6, and "crystal" translates *qerach*.
- **1:24** "Shaddai" translates *Shaddai*, a name of God, and "a sound of tumult" translates *qol hamullah*.
- **1:26** "Sapphire" translates *sappir*, and "a likeness with the appearance of a human being" translates *demut ke-mar'eh adam*. It is rendered as written.
- **1:27** "Gleaming metal" translates *chashmal*, and "a brightness" translates *nogah*.
- **1:28** "The bow" translates *qeshet*. "The glory of the LORD" translates *kevod YHWH*, and "I fell on my face" translates *va-eppol al-panai*.
- **2:1** "Son of man" translates *ben adam*, literally "son of a human being". The phrase is used of Ezekiel ninety-three times, and the rendering is open to the team's decision. See also Psalm 8:4 and Daniel 7:13.
- **2:2** "The spirit entered into me" translates *va-tavo-vi ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **2:3** "Nations of rebels" translates *goyim ha-moredim*, and "transgressed" translates *pasha'u*.
- **2:4** "Hard of face" translates *qeshei fanim*, and "stubborn of heart" translates *chizqei-lev*.
- **2:5** "A rebellious house" translates *beit meri*, as in 2:6-8, 3:9, 3:26-27 and elsewhere.
- **2:6** "Thistles" translates *sarabim*, a rare word, and "thorns" translates *sallonim*. "Scorpions" translates *aqrabbim*.
- **2:9** "A scroll" translates *megillat-sefer*.
- **2:10** "Lamentations and mourning and woe" translates *qinnim va-hegeh va-hi*.
- **3:3** "As sweet as honey" translates *ke-devash le-matoq*. See also Psalm 19:10, 119:103 and Revelation 10:9-10.
- **3:5** "Foreign speech" translates *amqei safah*, literally "deep of lip", and "a heavy tongue" translates *kivdei lashon*.
- **3:7** "Hard of forehead" translates *chizqei-metsach*.
- **3:9** "Diamond" translates *shamir*, a very hard stone, and "flint" translates *tsor*. See also Jeremiah 17:1.
- **3:12** "Blessed be the glory of the LORD" translates *barukh kevod-YHWH*. The Greek has a different reading, in which the glory "was lifted up from his place".
- **3:14** "Bitterness" translates *mar*, and "heat" translates *chamat*. "Spirit" is *ruach* in both places. See entry H-RUACH.
- **3:15** "Tel-abib" translates *Tel Aviv*, a place on the Chebar, and "overwhelmed" translates *mashmim*.
- **3:17** "A watchman" translates *tsofeh*. See also 33:1-9.
- **3:18** "To keep him alive" translates *lehachayyoto*, and is not *nefesh*. The text is not tagged.
- **3:19** "Turn" translates *ve-lo-shav*. See entry H-SHUV. "You will have saved your life" translates *nafshekha hitsalta*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **3:20** "A righteous person" translates *tsaddiq*, and "his righteousness" translates *tsidqo*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Turns" translates *bashuv*. See entry H-SHUV. "A stumbling block" translates *mikhshol*.
- **3:21** "You will have saved your life" translates *nafshekha hitsalta*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **3:22** "The valley" translates *ha-biq'ah*.
- **3:25** "Cords" translates *avotim*.
- **3:26** "Mute" translates *ilem*, and "to reprove them" translates *le-ish mokhiach*.
- **4:1** "A brick" translates *levenah*, and "engrave" translates *chaqota*.
- **4:2** "Siegeworks" translates *dayeq*, "a siege wall" translates *solelah*, and "battering rams" translates *karim*.
- **4:3** "An iron plate" translates *machavat barzel*.
- **4:4** "You will bear their iniquity" translates *ve-nasata et-avonam*.
- **4:5** "Three hundred and ninety days" is as the Hebrew has it. The Greek has "a hundred and ninety". The numbers of 4:5 and 4:6 are not explained in the Hebrew.
- **4:6** "A day for each year" translates *yom la-shanah yom la-shanah*.
- **4:8** "Cords" translates *avotim*, as in 3:25.
- **4:9** The grains are *chittim* (wheat), *se'orim* (barley), *pol* (beans), *adashim* (lentils), *dochan* (millet) and *kussemet* (emmer).
- **4:11** "The sixth part of a hin" translates *shishit ha-hin*.
- **4:12** "Human dung" translates *gelelei tsetat ha-adam*.
- **4:13** "Unclean" translates *tame*.
- **4:14** "My soul has not been defiled" translates *nafshi lo mtumma'ah*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Foul meat" translates *piggul*. See also Leviticus 17:15 and 19:7.
- **4:15** "Cow dung" translates *tselel ha-baqar*.
- **4:16** "The staff of bread" translates *matteh-lechem*, as in 5:16, Leviticus 26:26 and Psalm 105:16.
- **4:17** "Waste away" translates *namaqqu*.
- **5:1** "A barber's razor" translates *ta'ar ha-gallavim*, and "scales for weighing" translates *moznei mishqal*.
- **5:2** "The wind" translates *la-ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **5:3** "The skirts of your robe" translates *kenafeikha*.
- **5:5** "In the centre of the nations" translates *betokh ha-goyim*.
- **5:6** "My rules" translates *mishpatai*, and "my statutes" translates *chuqqotai*.
- **5:7** "More turbulent" translates *hamonekhem*, which is hard, and the Greek reads "more rebellious". "Have acted according to the rules of the nations" is as the Hebrew text has it. The Greek and the Syriac have "have not acted", and see 11:12.
- **5:10** "Fathers will eat their sons in your midst" is rendered as written, with no comment. See also Leviticus 26:29, Deuteronomy 28:53-57 and Jeremiah 19:9.
- **5:11** "My sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "I will withdraw" translates *egra*, which is hard and may be read "I will cut off".
- **5:13** "Satisfy myself" translates *ve-hinnachamti*. See entry H-NACHAM. "My jealousy" translates *qin'ati*.
- **5:15** "A reproach and a taunt, a warning and a horror" translates *cherpah u-gidduf musar u-mesham'mah*.
- **5:16** "The deadly arrows of famine" translates *chitsei ha-ra'av ha-ra'im*.
- **5:17** "Will bereave you" translates *shikkelu*.
- **6:2** "The mountains of Israel" translates *harei Yisra'el*.
- **6:3** "Ravines" translates *afiqim*, and "high places" translates *bamot*.
- **6:4** "Incense stands" translates *chammanim*, and "idols" translates *gillulim*, a word that means "dung gods".
- **6:9** "I was broken" is as the Hebrew has it (*nishbarti*), and the Greek has "I have broken". "Their whoring heart" translates *libbam ha-zoneh*, and "they will loathe themselves" translates *nuqotu*.
- **6:11** "Clap your hands and stamp your foot" translates *hakkeh be-khappekha u-rqa be-raglekha*.
- **6:13** "Pleasing aroma" translates *reach nichoach*, and "leafy oak" translates *elah avotah*.
- **6:14** "Diblah" is as the Hebrew has it. Many Hebrew manuscripts and the versions read "Riblah".
- **7:2** "An end" translates *qets*, and "the four corners" translates *arba kanfot*. See also Amos 8:2.
- **7:7** "Your doom" translates *ha-tsefirah*, a rare word that is hard.
- **7:10** "The rod has blossomed. Pride has budded." translates *tsats ha-matteh fara ha-zadon*, which is hard.
- **7:11** "A rod of wickedness" translates *matteh rish'ah*. The rest of the verse is hard in the Hebrew.
- **7:13** "His life" translates *chayyato*, and is not *nefesh*. The text is not tagged. The verse is hard.
- **7:16** "Doves of the valleys" translates *yonei ha-ge'ayot*, which is hard.
- **7:18** "Baldness" translates *qorchah*.
- **7:19** "Their hunger" translates *nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "The stumbling block of their iniquity" translates *mikhshol avonam*.
- **7:20** "His beautiful ornament" translates *tsevi edyo*, which is hard.
- **7:22** "My treasured place" translates *tsefuni*, which is hard.
- **7:23** "The chain" translates *ha-rattoq*, a rare word, and "bloody crimes" translates *mishpat damim*.
- **7:24** "Their holy places" translates *miqdeshehem*. See entry H-QADOSH. The verse is hard.
- **7:25** "Anguish" translates *qefadah*, and "peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **7:26** "The teaching" translates *torah*. See entry H-TORAH. "A vision" translates *chazon*.
- **7:27** "Is wrapped in despair" translates *yilbash shemamah*, and "terror" translates *behalot*.
- **8:1** "The sixth year ... on the fifth day of the sixth month" is September 592 before Christ. "The hand of the Lord GOD fell on me" translates *va-tippol alai yad Adonai YHWH*.
- **8:2** "The appearance of a man" follows the Greek and the Syriac (*mar'eh ish*). The Hebrew text has *mar'eh esh*, "the appearance of fire". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **8:3** "The form of a hand" translates *tavnit yad*, "a lock of my head" translates *tsitsit roshi*, and "the image of jealousy" translates *semel ha-qin'ah*.
- **8:5** "The altar gate" translates *sha'ar ha-mizbeach*.
- **8:6** "My sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "To drive me far" translates *le-rochqah*.
- **8:10** "Creeping thing" translates *remes*, "loathsome beast" translates *behemah sheqets*, and "idols" translates *gillulei*, a word that means "dung gods".
- **8:11** "Jaazaniah the son of Shaphan" is not the Jaazaniah of 11:1. "His censer" translates *miqtarto*.
- **8:12** "His room of pictures" translates *chadrei maskito*.
- **8:14** "Tammuz" translates *ha-Tammuz*, a god of the Babylonians.
- **8:16** "The porch" translates *ulam*, and "bowing down" translates *mishtachavitem*.
- **8:17** "They put the branch to their nose" translates *shollechim et-ha-zemorah el-appam*, which is hard and is traditionally read as a scribal change. "Provoke me again" translates *va-yashuvu le-hakh'iseni*, in which the verb *shuv* is used with another verb. The text is not tagged.
- **9:1** "The executioners of the city" translates *pequddot ha-ir*, and "weapon of destruction" translates *kli mashchato*.
- **9:2** "Linen" translates *baddim*, and "a writing case" translates *qeset ha-sofer*.
- **9:3** "The threshold" translates *miftan*.
- **9:4** "A mark" translates *tav*, which is also the name of the last letter of the alphabet. See also Revelation 7:3 and 9:4. "Groan and sigh" translates *ha-nenchaqim ve-ha-ne'enachim*.
- **9:6** "My sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH. The verses 5 to 7 are rendered as written, with no comment.
- **9:8** "The pouring out of your wrath" translates *be-shofkhekha*.
- **9:9** "Injustice" translates *muteh*, which is hard.
- **10:1** "The dome" translates *raqia*, as in 1:22.
- **10:2** "The whirling wheels" translates *ha-galgal*, and "burning coals" translates *gachalei-esh*.
- **10:5** "God Almighty" translates *El Shaddai*.
- **10:9** "A wheel" translates *ofan*, and "beryl" translates *tarshish*, as in 1:15-16.
- **10:12** The verse is hard, and "their backs" may be read differently.
- **10:13** "The whirling wheels" translates *ha-galgal*, which is not the word *ofan* of 1:15.
- **10:14** "The face of a cherub" translates *penei ha-keruv*. In 1:10 the face is the face of an ox (*shor*).
- **10:15** "Cherubim" translates *keruvim*. They are the living creatures of 1:5 (see 10:20).
- **10:17** "The spirit of the living creature" translates *ruach ha-chayyah*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **11:1** "Jaazaniah the son of Azzur" is not the Jaazaniah of 8:11. "The spirit" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **11:3** "The time is not near to build houses" translates *lo be-qarov benot batim*, which is hard. "The cooking pot" translates *ha-sir*, and "the meat" translates *ha-basar*.
- **11:5** "The Spirit of the LORD" translates *ruach YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH. The capital letter is the draft's choice, and the team should decide it with Isaiah 63:10-11.
- **11:12** The verse is close to 5:7, and the Hebrew text here has "have acted according to the rules of the nations".
- **11:13** "A full end" translates *kalah*.
- **11:16** "A sanctuary" translates *le-miqdash*. See entry H-QADOSH. "For a little while" translates *me'at*. The verse is hard.
- **11:19** "One heart" is as the Hebrew has it (*lev echad*). Many Hebrew manuscripts and the versions read "a new heart" (*lev chadash*), as in 36:26. "A new spirit" translates *ruach chadashah*. See entry H-RUACH. "The heart of stone" translates *lev ha-even*, and "a heart of flesh" translates *lev basar*.
- **11:21** "Detestable things" translates *shiqqutseihem*.
- **11:23** "The mountain that is on the east side of the city" is the Mount of Olives (see also Zechariah 14:4).
- **11:24** "The Spirit of God" translates *ruach Elohim*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **12:2** "A rebellious house" translates *beit meri*, as in 2:5.
- **12:3** "An exile's baggage" translates *kelei golah*.
- **12:6** "At dusk" translates *ba-alatah*, a rare word.
- **12:10** "This oracle" translates *ha-massa ha-zeh*, and "the prince" translates *ha-nasi*, who is Zedekiah.
- **12:13** "He will not see it" translates *ve-otah lo yireh*. See also 2 Kings 25:7 and Jeremiah 52:11.
- **12:14** "Every wind" translates *le-khol-ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **12:18** "Quaking" translates *ra'ash*.
- **12:22** "A proverb" translates *mashal*, and "every vision comes to nothing" translates *avad kol-chazon*.
- **12:24** "Flattering divination" translates *miqsam chalaq*.
- **13:2** "From their own hearts" translates *mi-libbam*.
- **13:3** "Their own spirit" translates *et-ruchan*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **13:4** "Jackals" translates *shu'alim*.
- **13:5** "The breaches" translates *ha-perutsot*.
- **13:9** "The register" translates *ketav*, literally "the writing".
- **13:10** "Peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Smear it with whitewash" translates *tafelim otah tafel*, in which the word *tafel* is rare and may mean "plaster".
- **13:11** "A stormy wind" translates *ruach se'arot*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **13:18** "Magic bands" translates *kessatot*, and "veils" translates *mispachot*. The words are rare. "In the hunt for souls" translates *le-tsodedot nefashot*, and the word *nefesh* is also used in 13:19 and 13:20. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **13:19** "Handfuls of barley" translates *shoalei se'orim*.
- **13:22** "The righteous" translates *tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Turn" translates *mi-shuvo*. See entry H-SHUV. "To keep him alive" translates *lehachayyoto*, and is not *nefesh*.
- **14:3** "The stumbling block of their iniquity" translates *mikhshol avonam*, "idols" translates *gillulim*, and "be consulted" translates *ida'resh*.
- **14:6** "Return and turn away ... turn away" translates *shuvu ve-hashivu ... hashivu*, three forms of *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **14:9** "Is deceived" translates *yefutteh*, and "I have deceived" translates *pittetti*. The verse is hard.
- **14:14** "Noah, Daniel and Job" translate *Noach Dani'el ve-Iyyov*. The Daniel here is probably an ancient figure and not the Daniel of the book of that name (see also 28:3). "Their own lives" translates *nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "By their righteousness" translates *be-tsidqatam*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **14:21** "My four disastrous judgements" translates *arba'at shefatai ha-ra'im*.
- **14:22** "You will be consoled" translates *ve-nichamtem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **15:2** "The wood of the vine" translates *etz ha-gefen*.
- **15:4** "Fuel" translates *okhlah*.
- **15:8** "They have acted faithlessly" translates *ma'alu ma'al*.
- **16:2** "Her abominations" translates *to'avoteha*.
- **16:3** "Your origin" translates *mekhorotayikh*, and "your birth" translates *moldotayikh*. "An Amorite" and "a Hittite" are peoples of the land before Israel.
- **16:4** "Your cord" translates *mokharotekh*, which is hard, and "wrapped in cloths" translates *hachtel lo chuttalt*.
- **16:6** "Wallowing" translates *mitbosesset*, and "Live" translates *chayi*.
- **16:7** "Full adornment" translates *ba'i ba'adi adayim*, which is hard.
- **16:8** "At the age for love" translates *et dodim*. "I spread the corner of my garment over you" translates *va-eferos kenafi alayikh*. See also Ruth 3:9. "A covenant" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **16:13** "Royalty" translates *limlukhah*.
- **16:15** "Played the prostitute" translates *va-tizni*, and "lavished" translates *va-tishpekhi*, literally "poured out".
- **16:16** "Colourful shrines" translates *bamot tselu'ot*.
- **16:17** "Images of men" translates *tsalmei zakhar*.
- **16:20** "You sacrificed to them to be devoured" translates *va-tizbachim lahem le-okhlah*. It is rendered as written, with no comment. See also Leviticus 18:21, Jeremiah 7:31 and 19:5.
- **16:24** "A vaulted chamber" translates *gev*, and "a lofty place" translates *ramah*.
- **16:26** "Your lustful neighbours" translates *gidlei vasar*, literally "large of flesh".
- **16:27** "Your allotted portion" translates *choqekh*.
- **16:30** "How sick is your heart" translates *mah amulah libbatekh*, in which the word *amulah* is rare.
- **16:36** "Your lust" translates *nechushtekh*, literally "your copper", which is hard.
- **16:38** "The blood of wrath and jealousy" translates *dam chemah ve-qin'ah*.
- **16:44** "Like mother, like daughter" translates *ka-em bitah*.
- **16:49** "Pride, excess of food and prosperous ease" translates *ge'on sivat lechem ve-shalvat hashqet*. The word *shalvah* is ease, and is not *shalom*. It is rendered as written.
- **16:50** "An abominable thing" translates *to'evah*.
- **16:51** "*Appear righteous" translates va-tetsaddeqi*, from *tsadaq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **16:52** "They are more in the right" translates *tits'daqnah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **16:53** "I will restore their fortunes" translates *ve-shavti et-shevutehen*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **16:54** "A consolation" translates *be-nachamekh otan*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **16:55** "Will return" translates *tashuvenah*, and "their former estate" translates *le-qadmatan*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **16:57** "Syria" is as the Hebrew has it (*Aram*). Many Hebrew manuscripts read "Edom".
- **16:59** "The oath" translates *alah*, and "the covenant" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **16:60** "An everlasting covenant" translates *berit olam*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **16:63** "When I atone for you" translates *be-khapperi lakh*. See entry H-KAPPER. "Open your mouth" translates *pithon peh*.
- **17:2** "A riddle" translates *chidah*, and "a parable" translates *mashal*.
- **17:3** "A great eagle" translates *ha-nesher ha-gadol*, "pinions" translates *ever*, and "the top of the cedar" translates *tsammeret ha-erez*.
- **17:4** "A land of trade" translates *erets kena'an*, in which *kena'an* also means "merchants".
- **17:5** "A willow twig" translates *tsaftsafah*, which is hard.
- **17:10** "The east wind" translates *ruach ha-qadim*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **17:12** The king of Babylon took Jehoiachin to Babylon in 597 before Christ (see 2 Kings 24:10-17).
- **17:13** "One of the royal offspring" is Zedekiah. "A covenant" translates *berit*, and "oath" translates *alah*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **17:15** "His ambassadors" translates *mal'akhav*.
- **17:17** "Many lives" translates *nefashot rabbot*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **17:21** "Every wind" translates *le-khol-ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "The pick of his troops" translates *mivrachav*.
- **17:22** "A sprig" translates *tsammeret*, and "a tender one" translates *rakh*.
- **18:2** "The fathers have eaten sour grapes, and the children's teeth are set on edge" is as in Jeremiah 31:29, and the wording is matched. "Sour grapes" translates *boser*.
- **18:4** "All souls ... the soul" translates *kol-ha-nefashot ... ha-nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **18:5** "Righteous" translates *tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **18:6** "Her time of uncleanness" translates *nidat tum'atah*.
- **18:7** "A pledge" translates *chavolato*, and "robbery" translates *gezelah*.
- **18:8** "Interest" translates *neshekh*, and "profit" translates *tarbit*.
- **18:20** "The soul" translates *ha-nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "The righteousness of the righteous" translates *tsidqat ha-tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **18:21** "Turns away" translates *yashuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **18:23** "Turn" translates *be-shuvo*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **18:27** "He will save his life" translates *nafsho yechayyeh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **18:30** "Return and turn away" translates *shuvu ve-hashivu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **18:31** "A new heart and a new spirit" translates *lev chadash ve-ruach chadashah*. See entry H-RUACH. The words are as in 36:26, where 11:19 has "one heart".
- **18:32** "Turn" translates *hashivu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **19:1** "A lamentation" translates *qinah*, and "the princes" translates *nesi'ei*.
- **19:2** "A lioness" translates *lavi'ah*, and "young lions" translates *kefirim*. The lions and the vine are figures of the kings of Judah, and the footnote does not name them.
- **19:4** "Hooks" translates *chachim*.
- **19:5** "Her hope" translates *tiqvatah*.
- **19:9** "A cage" translates *sugar*.
- **19:10** "Like a vine in a vineyard planted by the water" translates *ke-gefen be-damekh al-mayim shetulah*, in which *be-damekh* ("in your blood") is hard.
- **19:11** "Rulers' sceptres" translates *shivtei moshelim*.
- **19:12** "The east wind" translates *ruach ha-qadim*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **19:14** "This is a lamentation and has become a lamentation" translates *qinah hi va-tehi le-qinah*.
- **20:1** "The seventh year ... in the fifth month, on the tenth day" is August 591 before Christ. "To consult" translates *lidrosh*.
- **20:3** "I will not be consulted" translates *im-iddaresh lakhem*.
- **20:5** "I chose" translates *bachari*, and "I swore" translates *va-essa yadi*, literally "I lifted up my hand".
- **20:6** "The glory of all lands" translates *tsevi hi le-khol-ha-aratsot*.
- **20:7** "The detestable things" translates *shiqqutsei einav*, and "the idols" translates *gillulei Mitsrayim*, a word that means "dung gods".
- **20:11** "By which a person will live if he keeps them" translates *asher ya'aseh otam ha-adam va-chai bahem*. See also Leviticus 18:5.
- **20:12** "Sanctify" translates *meqaddishkhem*. See entry H-QADOSH. "My Sabbaths" translates *shabbetotai*.
- **20:20** "Keep holy" translates *qaddeshu*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **20:25** "Statutes that were not good" translates *chuqqim lo tovim*, and the verses 25 and 26 are hard. They are rendered as written, with no comment.
- **20:26** "All their firstborn" translates *kol-peter rachem*, and "I might devastate them" translates *asher ashimmem*. See also Exodus 13:12 and 22:29.
- **20:28** "The provoking offering of their offerings" translates *ka'as qorbanam*.
- **20:29** "Bamah" translates *bamah*, "high place", in a play on the word *ba'im* ("you go").
- **20:31** "Make your sons pass through the fire" translates *ha'avir beneikhem ba-esh*.
- **20:33** "I will be king" translates *emlokh*.
- **20:37** "I will make you pass under the rod" translates *ha'avarti etkhem tachat ha-shavet*. "The bond of the covenant" translates *masoret ha-berit*, in which *masoret* is rare. See entry H-BERIT.
- **20:39** "My holy name" translates *shem qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **20:40** "My holy mountain" translates *har qodshi*, "your contributions" translates *terumoteikhem*, and "your sacred offerings" translates *kol-qodsheikhem*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **20:41** "I will show my holiness" translates *ve-niqdashti*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **20:45** The verses 45 to 49 are Hebrew 21:1-5, and so the English chapter 21 is Hebrew 21:6-37.
- **20:46** "Preach" translates *hatef*, and "the forest land in the Negeb" translates *ya'ar sedeh ha-Negev*.
- **21:1** The verse is Hebrew 21:6.
- **21:2** "The sanctuaries" translates *miqdashim*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **21:3** "Righteous and wicked" translates *tsaddiq ve-rasha*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **21:6** "With breaking heart" translates *be-shivron motnayim*, literally "with the breaking of the loins".
- **21:7** "Spirit" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **21:10** "The rod of my son, it despises every tree" is hard in the Hebrew.
- **21:12** "Strike your thigh" translates *saphaq al-yarekh*.
- **21:13** "A testing" translates *bochan*, and the verse is hard.
- **21:15** "The glittering sword" translates *chereb baraq*, and "I have given" translates *natati*, which is hard.
- **21:17** "I will let my fury rest" translates *ve-hanichoti chamati*, from *nuach*, and is not *nacham*. The text is not tagged.
- **21:19** "A signpost" translates *yad*.
- **21:21** "The household gods" translates *ba-terafim*, and "he looks at the liver" translates *ra'ah ba-kaved*, a form of divination.
- **21:22** "Battering rams" translates *karim*, "ramps" translates *solelah*, and "siege towers" translates *dayeq*.
- **21:23** "Solemn oaths" translates *shevu'ot shevu'ot*, which is hard.
- **21:25** "Profane wicked one" translates *chalal rasha*.
- **21:26** "The turban" translates *ha-mitsnefet*, and "the crown" translates *ha-atarah*.
- **21:27** "A ruin, ruin, ruin" translates *avvah avvah avvah*. "Until he comes, the one to whom the judgement belongs" translates *ad-bo asher-lo ha-mishpat*, and the footnote does not decide how it is read. See also Genesis 49:10.
- **21:30** "Return it" translates *ha-shev*, from *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV. The verse may also be read as a question.
- **21:31** "Brutish" translates *ba'arim*.
- **22:2** "The bloody city" translates *ir ha-damim*.
- **22:3** "Idols" translates *gillulim*.
- **22:4** "The appointed time of your years" translates *ad shenotekha*.
- **22:5** "Full of tumult" translates *rabbat ha-mehumah*.
- **22:7** "The foreigner" translates *ger*.
- **22:8** "My holy things" translates *qodashai*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **22:9** "Slander" translates *rakhil*, and "lewdness" translates *zimmah*.
- **22:12** "Interest and profit" translates *neshekh ve-tarbit*.
- **22:18** "Dross" translates *sig*.
- **22:24** "A land that is not cleansed or rained on" translates *erets lo mutoharah hi lo gushmah*, which is hard.
- **22:25** "Her prophets" follows the Hebrew (*nevi'eha*), and the Greek has "her princes". "They have devoured lives" translates *nefesh akhalu*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **22:26** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH. "My holy things" translates *qodashai*, and "the holy and the common" translates *bein qodesh le-chol*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **22:27** "Destroying lives" translates *le-abbed nefashot*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **22:28** "Whitewash" translates *tafel*, as in 13:10.
- **22:30** "The breach" translates *perets*.
- **23:2** "Two women" translates *shetayim nashim*.
- **23:3** "They played the prostitute" translates *va-tiznenah*, and "their virgin bosoms" translates *shedei betuleihen*. The language of the chapter is rendered as written, with no comment. The rendering of *betulah* is open to the team's decision.
- **23:4** "Oholah" translates *Oholah*, "her tent", and "Oholibah" translates *Oholivah*, "my tent is in her".
- **23:5** The Assyrians are those of 2 Kings 15:19 and 17:1-23.
- **23:14** "Vermilion" translates *shashar*.
- **23:17** "She turned from them in disgust" translates *va-teqa nafshah mehem*, literally "her soul was alienated from them". See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **23:18** "I turned from her in disgust" translates *va-teqa nafshi me-aleha*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **23:20** "Her paramours" translates *pilagshehem*, and "members" translates *besaram*, literally "flesh". It is rendered as written.
- **23:22** "From whom you turned in disgust" translates *asher naqe'ah nafshekh me-aleihem*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **23:23** "Pekod and Shoa and Koa" are peoples east of the Tigris (see also Jeremiah 50:21).
- **23:24** "Wagons" translates *ofan*, and "buckler, shield and helmet" translates *tsinnah u-magen ve-qove*. The verse is hard.
- **23:25** "They will cut off your nose and your ears" is rendered as written, with no comment.
- **23:28** "From whom you turned in disgust" translates *asher naqe'ah nafshekh me-aleihem*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **23:32** "Deep and large" translates *amuqqah ve-rechavah*.
- **23:34** "Gnaw its broken pieces" translates *ve-garamt cheresh*.
- **23:35** "Bear the consequences of your lewdness" translates *se'i gam-at zimmatekh*.
- **23:37** "They have even offered up to them for food the children" translates *he'evaru lahem le-okhlah*. It is rendered as written, with no comment.
- **23:38** "My sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **23:40** "Painted your eyes" translates *kachalt einayikh*.
- **23:42** "A carefree multitude" translates *hamon sheleiv*, in which *sheleiv* means ease and is not *shalom*. "Drunkards" translates *sov'im*, and some manuscripts read "Sabeans" (*seva'im*). The verse is hard.
- **23:45** "Righteous people" translates *anashim tsaddiqim*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **23:49** "Your sinful idolatry" translates *chet'ei gillulekhen*.
- **24:1** "The ninth year, in the tenth month, on the tenth day" is January 588 before Christ (see also 2 Kings 25:1 and Jeremiah 52:4).
- **24:2** "Write down" translates *ketov-lekha*, and "this very day" translates *etsem ha-yom ha-zeh*.
- **24:3** "The pot" translates *ha-sir*.
- **24:6** "Corrosion" translates *chel'ah*, a rare word.
- **24:7** "The bare rock" translates *tsechiach sela*.
- **24:10** "Empty out the broth" translates *ve-harqach ha-merqachah*, which is hard.
- **24:11** "Its copper" translates *nechoshto*.
- **24:12** "She has wearied herself with toil" translates *te'unim hit'ah*, which is hard.
- **24:13** "Lewdness" translates *zimmah*.
- **24:14** "I will not relent" translates *lo anachem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **24:16** "The delight of your eyes" translates *machmad einekha*, who is the prophet's wife (see verse 18). "At a stroke" translates *be-maggefah*.
- **24:17** "Sigh" translates *he'anek*. "The bread of men" translates *lechem anashim*, which may be the bread that others bring to the mourners (see Hosea 9:4 and Jeremiah 16:7).
- **24:21** "My sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The pride of your power" translates *ge'on uzzekhem*, and "the yearning of your soul" translates *machmal nafshekhem*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **24:22** "The bread of men" is as in verse 17.
- **24:25** "Their stronghold" translates *ma'uzzam*, and "the desire of their soul" translates *masa nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **24:27** "Your mouth will be opened" translates *yippatach pikha*. See also 3:26-27 and 33:21-22.
- **25:3** "Aha" translates *he'ach*, and "my sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **25:4** "The people of the East" translates *benei-qedem*.
- **25:6** "With all the malice of your soul" translates *be-khol-she'tkha be-nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **25:9** "The flank of Moab" translates *ketef Mo'av*. "Beth-jeshimoth", "Baal-meon" and "Kiriathaim" are cities of Moab.
- **25:15** "With malice of soul" translates *bi-she'at nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **25:16** "The Cherethites" translate *Keretim*, a people of Crete or of the coast.
- **26:1** "The eleventh year" is 587 or 586 before Christ, and the month is not given in the Hebrew text.
- **26:2** "Tyre" translates *Tsor*, and "the gate of the peoples" translates *daltot ha-ammim*.
- **26:4** "A bare rock" translates *tsechiach sela*.
- **26:7** "King of kings" translates *melekh melakhim*.
- **26:8** "A siege wall" translates *dayeq*, "a ramp" translates *solelah*, and "a roof of shields" translates *tsinnah*.
- **26:9** "The shock of his battering rams" translates *mechi qavvav*, which is hard.
- **26:17** The verse is hard in the Hebrew.
- **26:20** "The pit" translates *bor*, "the world below" translates *erets tachtiyyot*, and "the land of the living" translates *erets chayyim*.
- **27:2** "A lamentation" translates *qinah*.
- **27:3** "The entrances to the sea" translates *mevo'ot yam*, and "perfect in beauty" translates *kelilat yofi*.
- **27:5** "Senir" is the name of Mount Hermon.
- **27:6** "Pines" translates *ashurim*, which is hard, and "the coasts of Cyprus" translates *iyyei Kittim*.
- **27:7** "Elishah" is a land of the west.
- **27:9** "Gebal" is the city of Byblos.
- **27:10** "Persia", "Lud" and "Put" are peoples who served as soldiers.
- **27:11** "Helech" and "Gamad" are not known.
- **27:13** "Human lives" translates *nefesh adam*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Javan", "Tubal" and "Meshech" are peoples of the north and the west.
- **27:14** "Beth-togarmah" is a land of the north.
- **27:15** "The people of Dedan" follows the Hebrew, and the Greek has "the Rhodians". "Ivory tusks" translates *qarnot shen*, and "ebony" translates *hovnim*.
- **27:16** "Syria" follows the Hebrew (*Aram*), and many manuscripts read "Edom".
- **27:17** "Millet" translates *pannag*, which is rare, and "balm" translates *tsori*.
- **27:19** "Vedan" translates *Vedan*, which is hard.
- **27:24** The verse is hard in the Hebrew.
- **27:26** "The east wind" translates *ruach ha-qadim*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **27:31** "In bitterness of soul" translates *be-mar nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **27:36** "Hiss" translates *sharequ*, and "a dreadful end" translates *ballahot*.
- **28:2** "The prince" translates *nagid*, and "the seat of the gods" translates *moshav Elohim*.
- **28:3** "Daniel" translates *Dani'el*, as in 14:14.
- **28:10** "The uncircumcised" translates *arelim*.
- **28:12** "The signet of perfection" translates *chotem tokhnit*, which is hard.
- **28:13** The names of the stones (*odem*, *pitdah*, *yahalom*, *tarshish*, *shoham*, *yashpeh*, *sappir*, *nofekh* and *bareqet*) are given with the names of the English tradition, and the Hebrew names are uncertain. "Eden, the garden of God" translates *Eden gan Elohim*. It is rendered as written, with no comment on who is meant.
- **28:14** "An anointed guardian cherub" follows the Hebrew (*at-kerub mimshach ha-sokhekh*), and the Greek has "with the cherub". "The holy mountain of God" translates *har qodesh Elohim*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The stones of fire" translates *avnei esh*.
- **28:16** "I destroyed you, guardian cherub" translates *va-abbedkha kerub ha-sokhekh*, which may also be read "the guardian cherub destroyed you".
- **28:18** "Your sanctuaries" translates *miqdeshekha*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **28:22** "I will show my holiness" translates *ve-niqdashti*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **28:24** "A brier" translates *sillon*, and "a thorn" translates *qots*.
- **28:25** "I will show my holiness" translates *ve-niqdashti*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **29:1** "The tenth year, in the tenth month, on the twelfth day" is January 587 before Christ.
- **29:3** "The great dragon" translates *ha-tannim ha-gadol*, and "my Nile" translates *ye'ori*.
- **29:4** "Hooks" translates *chachim*, as in 19:4.
- **29:6** "A staff of reed" translates *mish'enet qaneh*. See also Isaiah 36:6.
- **29:10** "From Migdol to Syene" translates *mi-Migdol Seveneh*, from the north to the south of Egypt. "Cush" is the land south of Egypt.
- **29:11** "Forty years" is not explained in the Hebrew.
- **29:14** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ve-shavti et-shevut*, and "bring them back" translates *ve-hashivoti*. See entry H-SHUV. "Pathros" is Upper Egypt.
- **29:16** "Recalling their iniquity" translates *mazkir avon*, and "when they turn to them" translates *be-fnotam acharehem*, from *panah*, and is not *shuv*. The text is not tagged.
- **29:17** "The twenty-seventh year, in the first month, on the first day" is April 571 before Christ, the latest date in the book.
- **29:18** "Every head was made bald, and every shoulder was rubbed bare" describes the labour of the soldiers.
- **29:21** "A horn" translates *qeren*, and "I will open your lips" translates *pithon peh*.
- **30:2** "Alas for the day" translates *hah la-yom*.
- **30:3** "The day of the LORD" translates *yom la-YHWH*, and "a time of doom for the nations" translates *et goyim*.
- **30:5** "The people of the land that is in league" translates *benei erets ha-berit*, in which *berit* means an alliance. The text is not tagged, and the verse is hard.
- **30:9** "In ships" translates *ba-tsiyyim*, and "unsuspecting" translates *betach*.
- **30:13** "The idols" translates *gillulim*, and "the images" translates *elilim*. "Memphis" translates *Nof*.
- **30:14** "Zoan" translates *Tso'an*, and "Thebes" translates *No*.
- **30:15** "Pelusium" translates *Sin*.
- **30:17** "On" translates *Aven*, which is Heliopolis, and "Pi-beseth" is Bubastis.
- **30:18** "Tehaphnehes" translates *Tachpanches*, as in Jeremiah 43:7.
- **30:20** "The eleventh year, in the first month, on the seventh day" is April 587 before Christ.
- **30:21** "A bandage" translates *tachboshet*.
- **31:1** "The eleventh year, in the third month, on the first day" is June 587 before Christ.
- **31:3** "Assyria" follows the Hebrew (*Ashur*), and many read "a cypress" (*te'ashshur*). The verses 3 to 5 are hard.
- **31:8** "The garden of God" translates *gan-Elohim*, and "the plane trees" translates *armonim*. It is rendered as written, with no comment.
- **31:15** "Sheol" translates *She'olah*. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **31:16** "Were comforted" translates *va-yinnachamu*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **32:1** "The twelfth year, in the twelfth month, on the first day" is March 585 before Christ.
- **32:2** "A dragon" translates *tannim*.
- **32:10** "For his own life" translates *le-nafsho*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **32:17** "The twelfth year, in the first month, on the fifteenth day" follows the Greek for the month, and the Hebrew text does not give it.
- **32:18** "The world below" translates *erets tachtiyyot*.
- **32:21** "Sheol" translates *She'ol*. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **32:27** "And they do not lie with the fallen mighty" follows the Hebrew (*ve-lo yishkevu*), and the Greek has "they lie". The verse is hard.
- **32:31** "He will be comforted" translates *ve-nicham*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **33:2** "A watchman" translates *tsofeh*, and "the trumpet" translates *shofar*.
- **33:5** "He would have saved his life" translates *nafsho milet*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **33:8** "To turn" translates *le-hashiv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **33:9** "You will have saved your life" translates *nafshekha hitsalta*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **33:10** "We rot away" translates *nemaqqim*.
- **33:11** "Turn back, turn back" translates *shuvu shuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **33:12** "The righteousness of the righteous" translates *tsidqat ha-tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **33:15** "The statutes of life" translates *chuqqot ha-chayyim*.
- **33:21** "The twelfth year of our exile, in the tenth month, on the fifth day" is January 585 before Christ, as the Hebrew has it. Many manuscripts and the Syriac read "the eleventh year".
- **33:22** "He had opened my mouth" is as in 3:26-27 and 24:27.
- **33:24** "These waste places" translates *ha-chorvot ha-elleh*.
- **33:31** "Lustful talk" translates *agavim*, and "their gain" translates *betsam*. The verse is hard.
- **33:32** "Lustful songs" translates *shir agavim*, which is hard.
- **34:2** "Shepherds" translates *ro'im*, a word for the leaders and the kings.
- **34:4** "You have not brought back" translates *lo hashivotem*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **34:12** "A day of clouds and thick darkness" translates *yom anan va-arafel*.
- **34:16** "I will bring back" translates *ashiv*. See entry H-SHUV. "I will destroy" follows the Hebrew (*ashmid*), and the Greek, the Syriac and the Latin read "I will watch over" (*eshmor*).
- **34:21** "You push with side and shoulder" translates *be-tsad u-vechatef*.
- **34:23** "One shepherd, my servant David" is rendered as written, with no comment. See also 37:24.
- **34:25** "A covenant of peace" translates *berit shalom*. See entries H-BERIT and H-SHALOM. See also Isaiah 54:10.
- **34:26** "Showers of blessing" translates *geshmei berakhah*.
- **34:27** "The bars of their yoke" translates *motot ullam*.
- **34:29** "Renowned plantations" translates *matta le-shem*, which is hard, and the Greek has "a plantation of peace".
- **34:31** "Human" translates *adam*.
- **35:2** "Mount Seir" translates *Har Se'ir*, which stands for Edom.
- **35:5** "Perpetual enmity" translates *eivat olam*, and "the time of their final punishment" translates *et avon qets*.
- **35:10** "Although the LORD was there" translates *ve-YHWH sham hayah*.
- **35:14** "While the whole earth rejoices" translates *kismoach kol-ha-arets*, which is hard.
- **36:2** "The ancient heights" translates *bamot olam*.
- **36:3** "Evil gossip" translates *dibbat lashon*, which is hard.
- **36:5** "Wholehearted joy" translates *simchat kol-lev*.
- **36:20** "My holy name" translates *shem qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH. The same is in 36:21 and 36:22.
- **36:23** "I will show the holiness of my great name" translates *ve-qiddashti et-shemi ha-gadol*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **36:25** "I will sprinkle clean water on you" translates *ve-zaraqti alekhem mayim tehorim*.
- **36:26** "A new heart" translates *lev chadash*, "a new spirit" translates *ruach chadashah*, "the heart of stone" translates *lev ha-even*, and "a heart of flesh" translates *lev basar*. See entry H-RUACH. The words are as in 18:31, and in 11:19 the Hebrew has "one heart".
- **36:27** "My Spirit" translates *ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. The capital letter is the draft's choice, and the team should decide it with Isaiah 63:10-11.
- **36:29** "I will save you" translates *ve-hoshati etkhem*.
- **36:35** "The garden of Eden" translates *gan-Eden*.
- **36:38** "The flock for sacrifices" translates *tson qodashim*. The word *qodashim* means "sacred offerings", and the text is not tagged.
- **37:1** "The Spirit of the LORD" translates *be-ruach YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH. The capital letter is the draft's choice, as in 11:5 and 11:24.
- **37:2** "The valley" translates *ha-biq'ah*.
- **37:3** "Can these bones live" translates *ha-techyenah ha-atsamot ha-elleh*.
- **37:5** "Breath" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. The same word is used in 37:6, 37:8, 37:9 and 37:10, with "breath" in the verses on the bones and "winds" in 37:9 (*arba ruchot*).
- **37:7** "A rattling" translates *ra'ash*.
- **37:11** "Our bones are dried up, and our hope is lost. We are indeed cut off" translates *yavshu atsmotenu avdah tiqvatenu nigzarnu lanu*.
- **37:12** "Your graves" translates *qivrotekhem*.
- **37:14** "My Spirit" translates *ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **37:16** "A stick" translates *etz*.
- **37:22** "One nation" translates *goy echad*, and "one king" translates *melekh echad*.
- **37:23** "Dwelling places" is as the Hebrew has it (*moshevoteihem*). The Greek and the Syriac read "backslidings" (*meshuvoteihem*), from *shuv*. The text is not tagged.
- **37:24** "My servant David ... one shepherd" is rendered as written, with no comment. See also 34:23.
- **37:26** "A covenant of peace" and "an everlasting covenant" translate *berit shalom* and *berit olam*. See entries H-BERIT and H-SHALOM. "My sanctuary" translates *miqdashi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **37:28** "Sanctify" translates *meqaddesh*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **38:2** "Gog" and "Magog" are not known as places. "The chief prince of Meshech and Tubal" translates *nesi rosh Meshekh ve-Tuval*, in which *rosh* may also be read as a name ("the prince of Rosh, Meshech and Tubal").
- **38:4** "I will turn you around" translates *ve-shovavtikha*, a form of *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV. "Hooks" translates *chachim*.
- **38:5** "Persia, Cush and Put" are peoples at the edge of the world known to the book.
- **38:6** "Gomer" and "Beth-togarmah" are peoples of the north.
- **38:8** "After many days" translates *mi-yamim rabbim*, and "in the latter years" translates *be-acharit ha-shanim*. "A land that has recovered from war" translates *erets meshovevet me-cherev*, in which the participle is from *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **38:12** "The centre of the earth" translates *tabbur ha-arets*, literally "the navel of the earth".
- **38:13** "Sheba", "Dedan" and "Tarshish" are as in 27:12-22.
- **38:16** "I show my holiness" translates *be-hiqqadshi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **38:21** "A sword against Gog on all my mountains" translates *le-khol-harai cherev*, which is hard, and the Greek has "terror".
- **38:22** "Sulphur" translates *gofrit*.
- **39:2** "I will turn you about" translates *ve-shovavtikha*. See entry H-SHUV. "Drive you forward" translates *ve-shishe'tikha*, which is hard.
- **39:7** "My holy name" translates *shem qodshi*, and "the Holy One in Israel" translates *qadosh be-Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **39:11** "The Valley of the Travellers" translates *gei ha-ovrim*, and "Hamon-gog" translates *Hamon Gog*, "the multitude of Gog". The verses 11 to 16 are hard.
- **39:16** "Hamonah" is a name that means "multitude".
- **39:17** "A sacrificial feast" translates *zevach*. The verses 17 to 20 are rendered as written, with no comment.
- **39:25** "I will restore the fortunes" translates *ashiv et-shevut*. See entry H-SHUV. "My holy name" translates *shem qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **39:27** "I have brought them back" translates *be-shuvi otam*. See entry H-SHUV. "I have shown my holiness" translates *ve-niqdashti*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **39:29** "I pour out my Spirit" translates *shafakhti et-ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. The capital letter is the draft's choice. See also Joel 2:28-29.
